# G2-mac: Between Your Machines, in the macOS App Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Sub-project G2 on the Mac. A Mac that has "Visible on this network" turned on (off by default) can pair with a Mac or a Windows/Linux PC that runs NotchMeow (matching six-digit codes, "They match" on both), and then send files to it and receive files from it over the local network. The receiver sees an **Accept card** for every file, which is an alert like an approval and which nothing accepts but a click; an accepted file streams over a Noise-encrypted channel into `~/Downloads/NotchMeow/` with the quarantine attribute set. The shelf gets a "To a device" button. Settings gets a Devices section. A Debug-only fake peer on the loopback address lets one Mac try all of it.

**Architecture:** Every pure rule is a Swift port of the G2-0 contract (`docs/LINK-CONTRACT.md`, `tests/fixtures/link/`), replayed against the fixtures by a script of its own: the Noise layer on CryptoKit alone (`LinkNoise`), the wire shapes (`LinkFrames`, with `LinkBytes`), the file-name rules (`LinkName`), the pairing machines and clocks (`LinkPairing`) and the transfer machines (`LinkFlow`). On top of them sit the conversations (`LinkChannel`, `LinkPairRun`, `LinkTransferReceive`, `LinkTransferSend`: Swift 6 actors over a `LinkTransport` protocol, tested between two in-memory ends), the file sink and source (`LinkFiles`), the Keychain items (`LinkKeychain`) and the Network.framework layer (`LinkNetwork`: transport, listener with its Bonjour advert, browser). `LinkService` joins them to the app; its views take plain values. There is no listener and no advert until the user turns Visible on, the browser runs only while Send or Devices is open, and nothing polls.

**Tech Stack:** Swift 6 (strict concurrency), CryptoKit, Network.framework, Security (Keychain), SwiftUI, AppKit, XcodeGen, `swiftc` test programs, Bash. No new dependency.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-04-file-shelf-transfer-design.md`, revision 2026-10-14, sections "G2. Between your machines", "Checks", "Rounds", "Plans and order", "Risks" and "Done means". The contract is G2-0's: `docs/LINK-CONTRACT.md`, `tests/fixtures/link/` (`noise-vectors.json`, `pairing-cases.json`, `frames.json`, `flow-cases.json`, `filename-cases.json`, `reference.py`, `build_cases.py`) and `tests/link-crosscheck/` (the CryptoKit cross-check). The Mac code it plugs into is G1-mac's (`ShelfModel`, `ShelfContent`, `ShelfView`, `ShelfSend`), the format is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-14-g1-mac-shelf.md`'s and `2026-10-14-f1-mac-sidekick.md`'s. The Windows and Linux twin is a separate plan (G2-tauri); "Parity" below says where they differ.

**Base:** a new branch `g2/mac-link` in a git worktree, from `notchmeow/rebrand` at **`6adc5a4` or later** (`git merge-base --is-ancestor 6adc5a4 HEAD` must succeed): G2-0 (the link contract) and G1-mac (the shelf) are merged. This plan never edits `design/prototype/cat-core.js`, a fixture of `tests/fixtures/`, `docs/LINK-CONTRACT.md`, `shared/theme.json`, `UploadCanvasView.swift`, `UploadSequenceEngine.swift`, `UploadCatDriver.swift`, `CatFileRule.swift` or anything under `windows/`.

## Global Constraints

Every task's requirements include this section. Values are copied from the spec and from the contract; lines marked "(plan decision)" are choices this plan makes where they are silent, and are recorded again under "The owner's answers (2026-10-15)".

- **Swift 6, SwiftUI and AppKit, no third-party dependency.** The Noise layer is hand-written on **CryptoKit only** (Curve25519, ChaChaPoly, SHA256, HMAC). `Noise_XX_25519_ChaChaPoly_SHA256` pairs, `Noise_KK_25519_ChaChaPoly_SHA256` carries a transfer, both with the prologue `notchmeow-link/1`; a DH result of 32 zero bytes aborts; a message whose tag fails does not move the counter.
- **Every pure rule is replayed against `tests/fixtures/link/` by a script of its own** (`scripts/test-link-noise.sh`, `-name`, `-frames`, `-pairing`, `-flow`), like the shelf and sidekick ports. `tests/fixtures/link/reference.py` decides when a port and a fixture disagree.
- **Visible on this network is off by default.** Until the user turns it on in Settings → Devices there is no listener, no advert of the machine's name and no local-network prompt. Once on it stays on until turned off. Browsing runs only while Send or Devices is open. A machine must be visible to pair and to receive.
- **Pair once, accept each time.** Both screens show the same six digits (commit-then-reveal, `LinkPairing.code`); a key is stored only when both users said "They match". At most one pairing attempt every 30 s; the pairing window is 2 minutes. **An incoming pairing is allowed only while Settings → Devices is open on this Mac, or within 2 minutes after the user clicked "Pair a machine" on this Mac** (the owner's answer of 2026-10-15; `LinkPairing.Gate`, the window closes at exactly 120 s like the contract's clock edges). At any other time the hello is refused silently, like any other refused hello: nothing is sent, the connection closes, no card and no alert. **Only a click accepts an offer.** An offer unanswered for 2 minutes is declined by itself. The Accept card is an alert like an approval: it opens the island even when hidden, holds it open, and the desktop cat flies to the notch for it (`transferOffer` in `desktopAlert`).
- **Keys and paired machines live in the Keychain** (`link.static-key`, `link.device-id`, `link.peer.<id>`, `LinkKeychain`), never in a file, a default, a log or a fixture. **A Debug build keeps all of them under a Keychain service of its own**, `io.github.aikusan.notchmeow.debug`, chosen at compile time under `#if DEBUG && !APPSTORE`; Release and App Store builds use the normal `io.github.aikusan.notchmeow`; `scripts/check-app-binary.sh` proves the debug string is absent from Release and App Store builds. No test and no dev path ever writes under the real service name (the Keychain test uses a service made for its run). The paired map is read again at the moment a key is stored. Ids and keys are canonical lower-case hex, an `own_id` is omitted when there is none and never null.
- **Received files** go to `~/Downloads/NotchMeow/`, named by `LinkName.sanitize` and numbered by `LinkName.uniqueName`, created **exclusively** (`O_EXCL`, `O_NOFOLLOW`), never opened by the app, quarantined (set by hand in the GitHub build; the sandbox does it in the App Store build). Limits: 4 GiB per transfer, one active transfer per peer, no automatic resume, transfers end when the app quits. The received byte count must equal the offer; a transfer that does not complete leaves no file.
- **App Store build:** adds `com.apple.security.network.server` and `com.apple.security.files.downloads.read-write` to its entitlements, and both Info.plists get `NSLocalNetworkUsageDescription` (one text now covers the AI model servers and the link) and `NSBonjourServices` = `_notchmeow._tcp`.
- **0 % CPU when the island is hidden; nothing polls.** The listener waits on a socket and every change comes from a callback. No `Timer`, display link, `DispatchSourceTimer`, `asyncAfter` or event monitor in the link; the only sleeps are the pairing window, an offer's 2 minutes, the connect timeout and the 10 s handshake watchdog, each a one-shot that exists only while that thing is pending (`scripts/test-link-wiring.sh` counts them).
- **No telemetry.** The log (`link.log`) says only fixed sentences ("listening", "a pairing started", "a transfer ended"): no name, size, address or id.
- **Never restyle a shipping view.** New views follow the existing app style (`CardBackground`, the 12.5 pt buttons, grey 11 pt notes, Settings' `GroupBox` rows). Every edit to an existing file is listed in "File Structure" and is additive: a case in `IslandView` and its layout, a switch case, two `AppState` flags, two hold-open clauses, one alert flag, a Settings row, a button in the shelf's bar, a menu item.
- **Frozen values:** the Keychain accounts above and the two service names, the defaults keys `link.visible` and `link.deviceName`, the Bonjour type `_notchmeow._tcp` and its TXT keys `v`, `id`, `name`, the island view case `link`, the markers `NM_LINK_ENGINE_ACTIVE` (every build) and `NM_LINK_DEV_PEER` (Debug only), pill IDs, cat IDs, the contract strings (`nm-hook`, `nm.sock`, `nm_kind`, `nm_agent`) and the bundle identifiers. A marker must be **16 bytes or more**: Swift keeps a shorter string literal inside an instruction, where `strings` cannot find it in a Release build.
- **Never edit `NotchBuddy.xcodeproj` by hand.** A task that adds a Swift file to the app runs `cd NotchBuddy && xcodegen` before it builds and commits the regenerated `project.pbxproj` (and the regenerated `Info.plist` and `InfoAppStore.plist`, which are generated from `project.yml`).
- **No `UserDefaults` suite, and no write to `UserDefaults.standard` or `~/Library`, in any test or script.** The Keychain test uses a service name that exists only for its run and deletes its items again; `NM_SKIP_REAL_KEYCHAIN=1` skips it.
- **Nothing of Coucou's comes back**, and the names Coucou and Mochi appear in no new code (`scripts/check-no-leftovers.sh` stays green).
- **Models and commits** (CLAUDE.md): Haiku 5.5 first, Sonnet 5.5 only on a named trigger, never Opus or Fable. Each commit is local only, never pushed, lists only the files its task names, and ends with the `Co-Authored-By` line of the model that did the task: `Claude Haiku 5.5` for `nm-implementer-light` and `nm-implementer`, `Claude Sonnet 5.5` for `nm-implementer-deep`.
- **SUBAGENT RULES (put this block in every subagent brief):**
  1. Never launch, quit or replace any NotchMeow app, and above all never touch the one installed in `/Applications`. A Debug, Release or App Store **build** in the worktree (`CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO`) is fine and is only built. The scripts that draw a PNG (`render-link.sh`, `render-shelf.sh`) are not the app and may run. No task sends a hook event to the app's socket. **The test programs may open sockets on 127.0.0.1 only** (`test-link-network.sh`); nothing may connect to another address, and no task uses Bonjour.
  2. Install nothing. No network calls, no downloads (this plan needs neither `npm` nor `cargo`), no Docker.
  3. Use `trash`, never `rm -rf`, for anything you delete by hand; scratch files go under `${TMPDIR:-/tmp}` in a uniquely named folder. (The test scripts clean their own `mktemp` folders with `rm -rf` in a `trap`: that is the repo's convention, not a command an agent runs.)
  4. No `UserDefaults` suite and no write to `UserDefaults.standard` or `~/Library` in a test or script. The only Keychain items a task may add are the ones `scripts/test-link-keychain.sh` adds under its own test-only service name and deletes again. Never read the real `~/.claude`.
  5. Never edit `design/prototype/cat-core.js`, a fixture of `tests/fixtures/`, `docs/LINK-CONTRACT.md`, `shared/theme.json`, a generated theme file or anything under `windows/`.
  6. Never push. Commit only in a task's commit step, after checking the worktree and the branch.
  7. Haiku 5.5 for every task except Tasks 6, 7, 8 and 10 (Sonnet 5.5); never Opus or Fable.
  8. Never rename a frozen ID, and write no `Coucou` or `Mochi` name anywhere in code, comments, scripts or tests.
  9. A test program that can hang (every conversation test) has a watchdog in it; if a script runs longer than two minutes, stop it by its PID only and report.
  10. If a command is denied by a permission prompt, or the find text of an Edit block does not occur exactly once, stop and report it. Do not look for a workaround.

## Review Focus

The spec says what the link does. It is silent on these inputs and conditions, and each would bite a person first, most likely first. Each line names the test that pins it.

1. **A machine that is not paired, or a peer that keeps asking.** A stranger on the Wi-Fi opens a transfer, sends a pairing attempt every few seconds, or opens connections and says nothing. Expected: a pairing hello outside the gate (Devices closed and no Pair click in the last 2 minutes) is refused silently with no card and no alert, an unknown device id is closed without an answer, a second pairing attempt inside 30 s is refused, a replayed commitment is refused, a connection that has not finished its handshake in 10 s is cut and at most 8 wait at once, and one peer's second offer is declined as busy. Tests: Task 3 (the gate's edges, 119 999 ms open and exactly 120 s closed), Task 6 (`test-link-session.sh`: an incoming pairing outside the gate is refused with nothing sent and no card, inside it runs; a stranger's key, replay, busy), Task 7 (`test-link-network.sh`: the silent connection), Task 3 (the fixtures' `attempt-too-soon`, `commit-replayed`), Task 14 (wiring: the gated function is the only way in, Devices tells the service when it is open, the unknown id is closed).
2. **A hostile or clumsy file name, and a name that is already taken.** `../../x`, `CON`, a zero-width character, a name 1 000 bytes long, a file with the same name already in Downloads, a symbolic link with that name. Expected: the offer is declined as not acceptable or the name is rewritten by the sanitizer, the saved name is at most 200 bytes, nothing is overwritten and nothing is followed through a link. Tests: Task 2 (162 name cases and the fuzz), Task 5 (`LinkFileSink`: `O_EXCL`, numbering, a link with the wanted name, a file made between the listing and the create).
3. **The connection or a user ends the transfer half way.** The other Mac sleeps, the Wi-Fi drops, the sender cancels, the user clicks Cancel, the sender's file shrinks while it is read. Expected: both sides end, the partial file is deleted, the card says nothing was saved, and a transfer never "completes" with fewer bytes than offered. Tests: Task 6 (cancel in the middle, partial file gone), Task 3 (`short`, `order`, `overflow`).
4. **Turning Visible off while something is going on, a failed listener, a declined permission.** Expected: turning it off stops the listener and cancels what is running, a listener that cannot start says so in Devices with the way to the Local Network setting, and the declined prompt leaves the machine invisible rather than half on. Tests: Task 8 (the rendered Devices pictures, the wiring pins on `stopListening`), owner check 3 and 10.
5. **An Accept card nobody sees.** The island is hidden, folded, the user is in another app or the Mac is locked. Expected: the card opens the island and the desktop cat flies in, it is not folded by the idle timer, it is declined after 2 minutes, nothing is saved, and the island does not stay open for ever after it. Tests: Task 10 (`offerCases` replayed, the hold-open pins), Task 6 (an offer nobody answers expires and creates no file), owner check 6.

Foundation only. |

**Parity with Windows/Linux (G2-tauri), and what differs.** The bytes, the rules and the files are the contract's, so the two apps talk to each other. The Mac uses CryptoKit and `Network.framework` (the other app `snow` and `mdns-sd`), the Keychain (Credential Manager or Secret Service there), the quarantine attribute (mark-of-the-web on Windows, nothing on Linux), the Settings → Devices GroupBox (a section of `settings/` there) and a Debug panel for the fake peer (`?dev` there). The hardening of the listener (10 s handshake deadline, 8 pending) is not in the contract; the other app may choose its own.

**App Store build.** All of it is in the App Store build. The differences: no quarantine call (the sandbox sets it), and the two entitlements and plist keys. Only the Debug fake peer is compiled out of Release and App Store builds, and `scripts/check-app-binary.sh` proves it.

**Order of tasks.** The spec's stages: Tasks 1 to 5 are the pure rules and files; Tasks 6 and 7 the conversations and the network (discovery is the listener and the browser); Task 8 pairing, Settings → Devices and the cards (**owner checkpoint 1** is Task 9); Task 10 the Accept card as an alert; Task 11 the Send-menu integration; Task 12 the Debug fake peer; Task 13 entitlements, Info.plist and the binary check; Task 14 wiring pins, docs and CI. The whole-branch and security review follows, then **owner checkpoint 2** (Task 15). The cross-app Swift-and-Rust loopback check is Task 16 and **waits for G2-tauri**: Docker is down and the Rust side does not exist yet.

## Tasks and agents

CLAUDE.md: the lightest model that fits. **Haiku 5.5 first; Sonnet 5.5 only on a named trigger.** The code is given in full in every task and its tests prove it. **Tasks 6, 7, 8 and 10 are `nm-implementer-deep` (Sonnet 5.5, high): trigger 1**, platform APIs and concurrency the task must get right rather than copy (Swift 6 actors and `AsyncStream` conversations with ordering and cancellation; `Network.framework` listener, Bonjour advert, browser and connection state callbacks; a `@MainActor` service that joins them to the island, the Settings window and the Keychain; the alert plumbing in the island's window controller and the desktop cat). If a Haiku task's expected output differs and the cause is not obvious, or it fails twice, hand it to `nm-implementer-deep` (trigger 2) and say so in the ledger. Coding agents load `ponytail:ponytail`, `superpowers:test-driven-development` and `superpowers:verification-before-completion`; the deep ones also `superpowers:systematic-debugging`; reviewers load `ponytail:ponytail-review`. Ponytail never cuts what this plan asks for, nor tests, security or privacy rules.

## Execution

One worktree chat session runs the whole plan: branch `g2/mac-link`, model **Sonnet 5.5 at high effort** (trigger 5, and trigger 1 for Tasks 6, 7, 8 and 10: an app plan with a listener, new crypto, a Keychain and the island's alert path; `ARCHITECT.md`). Nothing is pushed. It runs in a worktree of its own, in parallel with the Windows and Linux twin (G2-tauri), which touches nothing under `NotchBuddy/`; the two coordinate on `docs/SPEC.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `ARCHITECT.md` only, in Task 14, and meet in Task 16.

**The session is started in three steps** (ARCHITECT.md), because a chip's first turn runs on the app's default model and a model change applies only from a session's next turn:

1. **Setup and stop.** The chip's prompt only sets up (it carries the owner's advance approval of the brief in step 3):

   > Run `git switch -c g2/mac-link notchmeow/rebrand`, then Task 0 of `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-15-g2-mac-link.md`: only its Steps 1 to 3 (the base checks: `git merge-base --is-ancestor 6adc5a4 HEAD` must succeed). Read `CLAUDE.md`, `ARCHITECT.md`, that plan, `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-04-file-shelf-transfer-design.md` and `docs/LINK-CONTRACT.md`. If a base check fails, say so and stop. Reply "ready" and stop. Do not start Task 1. The owner approves in advance the controller brief the main session will send as your next message.

2. **Model.** Once the owner starts the chip, the main session sets that session to Sonnet 5.5 at high effort (`set_session_model`, `set_session_effort`).
3. **Brief.** The main session then sends the controller brief as the next message.

**Controller brief** (what the main session sends once the model is set):

> Run `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-15-g2-mac-link.md` with `superpowers:subagent-driven-development`, task by task, on branch `g2/mac-link`. You are the controller: Sonnet 5.5 at high effort (CLAUDE.md triggers 1 and 5). Dispatch each task to the agent the plan's "Tasks and agents" table names: `nm-implementer` (Haiku 5.5, high) by default, `nm-implementer-light` for the tasks the table gives it, `nm-implementer-deep` for Tasks 6, 7, 8 and 10 and for a task Haiku failed twice (then the trigger goes in the ledger); then `nm-reviewer` after every task, fixing what the review finds before the next one; and `nm-reviewer-deep` once over the whole branch at the end, with the security pass. Never Opus or Fable. Rules for you and for every subagent (put the plan's SUBAGENT RULES block in every brief):
>
> - **Where you are.** Before every commit run `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` and `git branch --show-current`: the first must be this worktree's folder (under `.claude/worktrees/`), the second `g2/mac-link`. If either is wrong, stop and report. Commit with `git commit -F <file>`, never `-m`, with the trailer of the model that did the task. Commit once per task. **Never push.**
> - **Ledger.** Keep a manual ledger at `.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-15-g2-mac-link/progress.md`: the base commit (`git merge-base HEAD notchmeow/rebrand`), then one line per task with its agent, model, commit hash, test lines and review verdict, and any escalation to `nm-implementer-deep` with the trigger.
> - **Briefs are task-sized.** A brief carries one task: its text, the Global Constraints, the SUBAGENT RULES and the files it names. Never hand a subagent the whole plan.
> - **Edits are exact.** An Edit block's find text must occur exactly once in the file. **If it does not, stop and report** (the tree is not what the plan expects): never "fix" the find text or type a block from memory.
> - **Reviews** read `git diff "$(git merge-base HEAD notchmeow/rebrand)"...HEAD` (three dots), narrowed to the task's files, never `..` and never a diff against a moving working tree.
> - **Every shell block in the plan passes `bash -n` before it is run**, and a script you write passes it too.
> - **The installed app.** **Never launch, quit, replace or open the NotchMeow in `/Applications`, and never launch any NotchMeow build.** Debug, Release and App Store builds are built with `CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO` into `NotchBuddy/build/` of the worktree and only built; the pictures come from `scripts/render-link.sh` and the checks from the test scripts. Install and download nothing. The owner runs the real app in Task 15.

At owner checkpoint 1 (Task 9), at the end (Task 15), and whenever you stop (a refusal, a failed check, a find text that does not occur once, a question), send your report to it as well as writing it in your own chat, so the main session can double-check the work (its own clone, merge and full checks) before anything reaches the owner.

After the owner's reply run Tasks 10 to 14, dispatch `nm-reviewer-deep` over the whole branch, fix what it finds, and stop for checkpoint 2 (Task 15), with the pictures and the real-build checklist. Task 16 is not run: it waits for G2-tauri.

Before any follow-up turn (a fix round, a question, a resumed run), the main session checks the session is still on its configured model and effort.

## How to run this plan

**Blocks.** A code step is either **Create `path`** (the file's exact content) or **Edit `path`** (a find text that occurs exactly once, and its replacement). Apply an edit by replacing the find text with the replacement and nothing else; if the find text occurs zero or two times, stop and report: the tree is not what the plan expects. Never type a block from memory and never "improve" one. Every `Run:` line is run from the repository root (the worktree's top folder); `Expected: FAIL` and `Expected: PASS` say which way it must go. A test program's failing run prints `✗` lines and its last line is `N test(s) failed.`; a passing one ends `All tests passed.` A compile that cannot find a file prints `error opening input file` (a red run before the code exists).

**Mac commands.** The test programs are `swiftc` programs run through `scripts/test-*.sh`; none needs Xcode. A build is `cd NotchBuddy && xcodegen && cd ..` and then `xcodebuild -project NotchBuddy/NotchBuddy.xcodeproj -scheme NotchBuddy -configuration Debug -derivedDataPath NotchBuddy/build build CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO`; the Release build is `-configuration Release -derivedDataPath NotchBuddy/build/release` and the App Store target `-scheme NotchMeowAppStore -configuration Debug -derivedDataPath NotchBuddy/build/store`, each with `CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO CODE_SIGNING_REQUIRED=NO` (git ignores `NotchBuddy/build/`). Add `2>&1 | tail -3` to read the last lines. A build takes two minutes or more: run it in the background with its output in a file. **Never start a built app.**

**Looking at an image.** Several steps end with "look at it". The agent that ran the step opens the PNG with its file reader and says in one sentence what it sees against the thing named; a blank or broken image is a failed step. The owner's look is the checkpoint.

**Two things the dry run found that every Swift port here already handles, and a test pins:** a `Data` slice does not start at index 0 (a `Data` made by joining two pieces may be a slice of CryptoKit's buffer: write into a fresh `Data` and index it safely), and `JSONSerialization` drops a byte order mark at the start of a string value (the file-name fixture is read with `JSONDecoder`).

**Dry-run clones.** A throwaway clone made with `git clone --local --shared` has no Docker and needs none. Commit steps are not run in a clone.

### Task 1: The Noise layer on CryptoKit, against the vectors

**Agent:** `nm-implementer-light` (Haiku 5.5, medium). The code is given in full; the vectors prove it.

**Files:**
- Create: `NotchBuddy/Sources/App/LinkNoise.swift`, `tests/LinkNoiseTests.swift`, `scripts/test-link-noise.sh`
- Modify: `NotchBuddy/NotchBuddy.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` (regenerated)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `tests/fixtures/link/noise-vectors.json` (`prologue`, `vectors[]` of `{id, protocol_name, init_*, resp_*, handshake_hash, messages[]}`, `rejectCases[]` of `{name, vector, kind, message, offset?, by?, prologue?, respRemoteStatic?, failsAt, because?}`), `docs/LINK-CONTRACT.md` "On the wire" and "Notes for ports"; `tests/CatTestSupport.swift` (`CT`).
- Produces: `LinkNoise` (`prologue`, `maxMessage`, `tag`, `hkdf2`, `publicKey(of:)`, `privateKey(raw:)`, `dh`), `LinkCipherState` (`encrypt(ad:_:)`, `decrypt(ad:_:)`, `n`), `LinkPattern` (`.xx`, `.kk`), `LinkHandshake` (`init(pattern:initiator:prologue:s:rs:e:)`, `write`, `read`, `finish() -> (hash, send, receive)`, `remoteStatic`, `isDone`, `isMyTurn`, `staticPublic`), `LinkNoiseError`.

**Why hand-written.** CryptoKit has the primitives but no Noise. The spec chose a hand-written layer over a new dependency, and the risk it names (subtle crypto bugs) is why the four vectors, the twenty damaged-message cases and the size limits are all replayed, and why the whole-branch review has a security pass. The starting hash is the protocol name itself (both names are 32 bytes, so no padding); the nonce is 32 zero bits then the 64-bit message count, little-endian.

**The first thing the dry run found:** `box.ciphertext + box.tag` of an empty message is a **slice** of CryptoKit's buffer that does not start at index 0, and flipping a bit at offset 0 of it trapped. `encrypt` therefore builds a fresh `Data`; `read` re-bases whatever it is given.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test and its script**

The program replays the four vectors (every ciphertext and the handshake hash), the twenty `rejectCases` (the conversation must stop at `failsAt`, and the two zero-point cases must be refused as a low-order point), the size limits (65535 bytes in a message, one more refused, a refused message does not move the counter), the key checks and 24 random sessions.

**Create `tests/LinkNoiseTests.swift`**

````swift
import CryptoKit
import Foundation

// The Noise layer of the link (docs/LINK-CONTRACT.md): every vector of tests/fixtures/link/noise-vectors.json replayed against LinkNoise.swift, every damaged-message case, and the size limits.
// Run it through scripts/test-link-noise.sh.

func linkHex(_ s: String) -> Data {
    var d = Data(), i = s.startIndex
    while i < s.endIndex { let j = s.index(i, offsetBy: 2); d.append(UInt8(s[i..<j], radix: 16)!); i = j }
    return d
}
func linkHx(_ d: Data) -> String { d.map { String(format: "%02x", $0) }.joined() }

@main
enum LinkNoiseTests {
    enum Outcome: Equatable { case ok([String], String), fail(Int, LinkNoiseError) }

    /// `noise_run` of reference.py: plays a vector's whole conversation, both sides, in this process.
    /// A mutation damages what is delivered, never what is sent.
    static func run(_ v: [String: Any], _ m: [String: Any] = [:]) -> Outcome {
        let kind = m["kind"] as? String
        let pattern = LinkPattern(rawValue: String((v["protocol_name"] as! String).split(separator: "_")[1]))!
        let isk = try! LinkNoise.privateKey(raw: linkHex(v["init_static"] as! String)), rsk = try! LinkNoise.privateKey(raw: linkHex(v["resp_static"] as! String))
        let kk = pattern == .kk
        let iPro = linkHex((kind == "prologue" ? m["prologue"] : v["init_prologue"]) as! String)
        var i = try! LinkHandshake(pattern: pattern, initiator: true, prologue: iPro, s: isk, rs: kk ? LinkNoise.publicKey(of: rsk) : nil,
                                   e: try! LinkNoise.privateKey(raw: linkHex(v["init_ephemeral"] as! String)))
        let respRemote = kind == "static" ? linkHex(m["respRemoteStatic"] as! String) : LinkNoise.publicKey(of: isk)
        var r = try! LinkHandshake(pattern: pattern, initiator: false, prologue: linkHex(v["resp_prologue"] as! String), s: rsk, rs: kk ? respRemote : nil,
                                   e: try! LinkNoise.privateKey(raw: linkHex(v["resp_ephemeral"] as! String)))
        let nhs = pattern.messages.count
        var sent: [String] = [], hash = Data()
        var iSend = LinkCipherState(), iRecv = LinkCipherState(), rSend = LinkCipherState(), rRecv = LinkCipherState()
        for (n, msg) in (v["messages"] as! [[String: String]]).enumerated() {
            let payload = linkHex(msg["payload"]!)
            let toResp = n % 2 == 0
            let c: Data
            do {
                if n < nhs { c = toResp ? try i.write(payload) : try r.write(payload) }
                else { c = toResp ? try iSend.encrypt(ad: Data(), payload) : try rSend.encrypt(ad: Data(), payload) }
            } catch let e as LinkNoiseError { return .fail(n, e) } catch { fatalError() }
            sent.append(linkHx(c))
            var delivered = [c]
            if (m["message"] as? Int) == n {
                switch kind {
                case "flip": let o = m["offset"] as! Int; var d = c; d[o] ^= 1; delivered = [d]
                case "truncate": delivered = [c.prefix(c.count - (m["by"] as! Int))]
                case "low-order": delivered = [Data(count: 32) + c.dropFirst(32)]
                case "replay": delivered = [c, c]
                default: break
                }
            }
            if kind == "skip", (m["message"] as! Int) - 2 == n { delivered = [] }
            for d in delivered {
                let got: Data
                do {
                    if n < nhs { got = toResp ? try r.read(d) : try i.read(d) }
                    else { got = toResp ? try rRecv.decrypt(ad: Data(), d) : try iRecv.decrypt(ad: Data(), d) }
                } catch let e as LinkNoiseError { return .fail(n, e) } catch { fatalError() }
                if d == c && got != payload { return .fail(n, .decrypt) }
            }
            if n == nhs - 1 {
                let a = try! i.finish(), b = try! r.finish()
                if a.hash != b.hash { return .fail(n, .decrypt) }
                (iSend, iRecv, rSend, rRecv, hash) = (a.send, a.receive, b.send, b.receive, a.hash)
            }
        }
        return .ok(sent, linkHx(hash))
    }

    @MainActor static func main() {
        let f = CT.fixture("noise-vectors.json", dir: "link")
        CT.section("noise-vectors.json: every message and the handshake hash")
        let vectors = f["vectors"] as! [[String: Any]]
        CT.check("four vectors", vectors.map { $0["id"] as! String }.sorted(), ["kk-empty", "kk-link", "xx-empty", "xx-link"])
        CT.check("the prologue is notchmeow-link/1", linkHx(LinkNoise.prologue), f["prologue"] as! String)
        var byId: [String: [String: Any]] = [:]
        for v in vectors {
            byId[v["id"] as! String] = v
            let want = Outcome.ok((v["messages"] as! [[String: String]]).map { $0["ciphertext"]! }, v["handshake_hash"] as! String)
            CT.check("\(v["id"] as! String): every ciphertext and the hash are the vector's", run(v), want)
        }

        CT.section("rejectCases: a damaged conversation stops where the contract says")
        for c in f["rejectCases"] as! [[String: Any]] {
            var m = c; for k in ["name", "vector", "failsAt", "because"] { m[k] = nil }
            let got = run(byId[c["vector"] as! String]!, m)
            if case .fail(let at, let why) = got {
                CT.check(c["name"] as! String, at, c["failsAt"] as! Int)
                if c["because"] as? String == "low-order point" { CT.check("   refused as a low-order point", why, .lowOrderPoint) }
            } else { CT.checkTrue("\(c["name"] as! String): the conversation went through", false) }
        }

        CT.section("sizes, the counter, the key checks")
        let a0 = try! LinkNoise.privateKey(raw: Data(repeating: 1, count: 32)), b0 = try! LinkNoise.privateKey(raw: Data(repeating: 2, count: 32))
        var a = try! LinkHandshake(pattern: .kk, initiator: true, s: a0, rs: LinkNoise.publicKey(of: b0))
        var b = try! LinkHandshake(pattern: .kk, initiator: false, s: b0, rs: LinkNoise.publicKey(of: a0))
        _ = try! b.read(try! a.write()); _ = try! a.read(try! b.write())
        var aSend = try! a.finish().send, bRecv = try! b.finish().receive
        let big = try! aSend.encrypt(ad: Data(), Data(count: 65535 - 16))
        CT.check("a transport message of 65535 bytes opens", [big.count, (try! bRecv.decrypt(ad: Data(), big)).count], [65535, 65519])
        var tooBig = false
        do { _ = try aSend.encrypt(ad: Data(), Data(count: 65535 - 16 + 1)) } catch { tooBig = (error as? LinkNoiseError) == .tooLong }
        CT.check("one byte more is refused when sent, and the counter does not move", [tooBig, aSend.n == 1], [true, true])
        let next = try! aSend.encrypt(ad: Data(), Data("next".utf8))
        var hurt = next; hurt[0] ^= 1
        var refused = false
        do { _ = try bRecv.decrypt(ad: Data(), hurt) } catch { refused = (error as? LinkNoiseError) == .decrypt }
        CT.check("a damaged message is refused and the counter does not move", [refused, bRecv.n == 1], [true, true])
        CT.check("so the genuine next one still opens", String(decoding: try! bRecv.decrypt(ad: Data(), next), as: UTF8.self), "next")
        var shortBox = false
        do { _ = try bRecv.decrypt(ad: Data(), Data(count: 5)) } catch { shortBox = (error as? LinkNoiseError) == .decrypt }
        CT.checkTrue("a box shorter than a tag is refused, not trapped", shortBox)
        var kkNeeds = false
        do { _ = try LinkHandshake(pattern: .kk, initiator: true, s: a0) } catch { kkNeeds = (error as? LinkNoiseError) == .needsRemoteStatic }
        CT.checkTrue("KK without the peer's key is refused", kkNeeds)
        var badKey = false
        do { _ = try LinkNoise.dh(a0, Data(count: 31)) } catch { badKey = (error as? LinkNoiseError) == .invalidKey }
        CT.checkTrue("a public key of 31 bytes is refused", badKey)
        var xx = try! LinkHandshake(pattern: .xx, initiator: true, prologue: Data(), s: a0)
        var oversize = false
        do { _ = try xx.write(Data(count: 65535 - 31)) } catch { oversize = (error as? LinkNoiseError) == .tooLong }
        CT.checkTrue("a handshake message of 65536 bytes is refused", oversize)
        var notTurn = false
        do { _ = try xx.read(Data(count: 40)) } catch { notTurn = (error as? LinkNoiseError) == .notMyTurn }
        CT.checkTrue("reading on the writer's turn is refused", notTurn)

        CT.section("random sessions, both patterns")
        var ok = true
        for n in 0..<24 {
            let pattern: LinkPattern = n % 2 == 0 ? .xx : .kk
            let isk = Curve25519.KeyAgreement.PrivateKey(), rsk = Curve25519.KeyAgreement.PrivateKey()
            let pro = Data((0..<Int.random(in: 0..<20)).map { _ in UInt8.random(in: 0...255) })
            var i = try! LinkHandshake(pattern: pattern, initiator: true, prologue: pro, s: isk, rs: pattern == .kk ? LinkNoise.publicKey(of: rsk) : nil)
            var r = try! LinkHandshake(pattern: pattern, initiator: false, prologue: pro, s: rsk, rs: pattern == .kk ? LinkNoise.publicKey(of: isk) : nil)
            while !i.isDone {
                let p = Data((0..<Int.random(in: 0..<80)).map { _ in UInt8.random(in: 0...255) })
                if i.isMyTurn { ok = ok && (try! r.read(try! i.write(p))) == p } else { ok = ok && (try! i.read(try! r.write(p))) == p }
            }
            let fi = try! i.finish(), fr = try! r.finish()
            ok = ok && fi.hash == fr.hash && i.remoteStatic == LinkNoise.publicKey(of: rsk) && r.remoteStatic == LinkNoise.publicKey(of: isk)
            var send = fi.send, recv = fr.receive
            let p = Data((0..<Int.random(in: 1..<300)).map { _ in UInt8.random(in: 0...255) })
            let c = try! send.encrypt(ad: Data(), p)
            var d = c; d[Int.random(in: 0..<c.count)] ^= UInt8(1 << Int.random(in: 0..<8))
            ok = ok && (try? recv.decrypt(ad: Data(), d)) == nil && (try? recv.decrypt(ad: Data(), c)) == p
        }
        CT.checkTrue("24 sessions: the hashes agree, each side learns the other's key, any damaged bit is refused", ok)
        CT.finish()
    }
}
````

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the implementation**

**Create `NotchBuddy/Sources/App/LinkNoise.swift`**

````swift
import CryptoKit
import Foundation

// The Noise layer of the link (sub-project G2, docs/LINK-CONTRACT.md "On the wire" and "Notes for ports"): `Noise_XX_25519_ChaChaPoly_SHA256` for a pairing and
// `Noise_KK_25519_ChaChaPoly_SHA256` for a transfer, revision 34, written on CryptoKit alone (Curve25519, ChaChaPoly, SHA256, HMAC). No third-party code. Foundation and CryptoKit only,
// so a test program can compile it. Replayed against tests/fixtures/link/noise-vectors.json by scripts/test-link-noise.sh; tests/fixtures/link/reference.py decides when they disagree.
//
// Value types only: a handshake and a cipher state are structs, so an actor or a connection owns its copy and nothing is shared. Nothing here logs, and no key leaves this file except
// through `LinkHandshake.remoteStatic` (the public key the peer proved) and the two cipher states `finish()` returns.

enum LinkNoiseError: Error, Equatable {
    case tooLong            // a Noise message is 65535 bytes at most, tag included
    case decrypt            // the tag failed (or the box was too short to hold one)
    case lowOrderPoint      // a DH output of 32 zero bytes (RFC 7748, section 6.1)
    case invalidKey         // a public key that is not 32 bytes, or a private key CryptoKit refuses
    case notMyTurn
    case short              // a handshake message too short for the tokens it must hold
    case notFinished
    case needsRemoteStatic  // KK before the first message needs the peer's static key
    case nonceExhausted
}

enum LinkNoise {
    static let prologue = Data("notchmeow-link/1".utf8)
    static let maxMessage = 65535
    static let tag = 16

    static func hmac(_ key: Data, _ message: Data) -> Data {
        Data(HMAC<SHA256>.authenticationCode(for: message, using: SymmetricKey(data: key)))
    }

    /// The two-output HKDF of the Noise spec.
    static func hkdf2(_ chainingKey: Data, _ input: Data) -> (Data, Data) {
        let temp = hmac(chainingKey, input)
        let out1 = hmac(temp, Data([1]))
        let out2 = hmac(temp, out1 + Data([2]))
        return (out1, out2)
    }

    static func sha256(_ data: Data) -> Data { Data(SHA256.hash(data: data)) }

    /// The public key of a private one (32 bytes each).
    static func publicKey(of privateKey: Curve25519.KeyAgreement.PrivateKey) -> Data { privateKey.publicKey.rawRepresentation }

    static func privateKey(raw: Data) throws -> Curve25519.KeyAgreement.PrivateKey {
        guard raw.count == 32, let k = try? Curve25519.KeyAgreement.PrivateKey(rawRepresentation: raw) else { throw LinkNoiseError.invalidKey }
        return k
    }

    /// X25519. A public key that is not 32 bytes is refused, and so is an all-zero output.
    static func dh(_ priv: Curve25519.KeyAgreement.PrivateKey, _ pub: Data) throws -> Data {
        guard pub.count == 32, let p = try? Curve25519.KeyAgreement.PublicKey(rawRepresentation: pub) else { throw LinkNoiseError.invalidKey }
        // CryptoKit may refuse a low-order point itself; either way it is the same refusal.
        guard let secret = try? priv.sharedSecretFromKeyAgreement(with: p) else { throw LinkNoiseError.lowOrderPoint }
        let out = secret.withUnsafeBytes { Data($0) }
        if out.allSatisfy({ $0 == 0 }) { throw LinkNoiseError.lowOrderPoint }
        return out
    }
}

/// ChaChaPoly with a 12-byte nonce of 32 zero bits and then the 64-bit message count, little-endian (not XChaCha).
struct LinkCipherState {
    private(set) var key: SymmetricKey?
    private(set) var n: UInt64 = 0

    init(key: Data? = nil) { self.key = key.map { SymmetricKey(data: $0) } }

    var hasKey: Bool { key != nil }

    private func nonce() throws -> ChaChaPoly.Nonce {
        var d = Data(count: 4)
        var le = n.littleEndian
        d.append(Data(bytes: &le, count: 8))
        return try ChaChaPoly.Nonce(data: d)
    }

    mutating func encrypt(ad: Data, _ plaintext: Data) throws -> Data {
        if plaintext.count + (key != nil ? LinkNoise.tag : 0) > LinkNoise.maxMessage { throw LinkNoiseError.tooLong }
        guard let key else { return plaintext }
        if n >= UInt64.max { throw LinkNoiseError.nonceExhausted }
        let box = try ChaChaPoly.seal(plaintext, using: key, nonce: nonce(), authenticating: ad)
        n += 1
        var sealed = Data(capacity: plaintext.count + LinkNoise.tag)   // a fresh Data starts at index 0, whatever slices CryptoKit hands back
        sealed.append(box.ciphertext)
        sealed.append(box.tag)
        return sealed
    }

    /// A message whose tag fails does not move the counter, so the genuine next one still opens.
    mutating func decrypt(ad: Data, _ ciphertext: Data) throws -> Data {
        if ciphertext.count > LinkNoise.maxMessage { throw LinkNoiseError.tooLong }
        guard let key else { return ciphertext }
        if n >= UInt64.max { throw LinkNoiseError.nonceExhausted }
        guard ciphertext.count >= LinkNoise.tag else { throw LinkNoiseError.decrypt }
        let body = ciphertext.prefix(ciphertext.count - LinkNoise.tag), tagBytes = ciphertext.suffix(LinkNoise.tag)
        guard let box = try? ChaChaPoly.SealedBox(nonce: nonce(), ciphertext: body, tag: tagBytes),
              let plain = try? ChaChaPoly.open(box, using: key, authenticating: ad) else { throw LinkNoiseError.decrypt }
        n += 1
        return plain
    }
}

enum LinkPattern: String {
    case xx = "XX", kk = "KK"

    var protocolName: String { "Noise_\(rawValue)_25519_ChaChaPoly_SHA256" }

    /// Tokens: `e` and `s` send a public key; `ee`, `es`, `se` and `ss` mix in a DH. In `es` the first letter is the initiator's key and the second the responder's.
    var messages: [[String]] {
        switch self {
        case .xx: return [["e"], ["e", "ee", "s", "es"], ["s", "se"]]
        case .kk: return [["e", "es", "ss"], ["e", "ee", "se"]]
        }
    }
    var preMessages: Bool { self == .kk }
}

/// One side of a handshake. `s` is the static private key, `rs` the peer's static public key (KK needs it before the first message), `e` an ephemeral private key (only the tests inject one;
/// an app leaves it nil and gets a random one at the first message).
struct LinkHandshake {
    private var h: Data
    private var ck: Data
    private var cs = LinkCipherState()
    private let pattern: LinkPattern
    private let initiator: Bool
    private let s: Curve25519.KeyAgreement.PrivateKey
    private var e: Curve25519.KeyAgreement.PrivateKey?
    private var re: Data?
    private(set) var remoteStatic: Data?
    private var index = 0

    init(pattern: LinkPattern, initiator: Bool, prologue: Data = LinkNoise.prologue, s: Curve25519.KeyAgreement.PrivateKey, rs: Data? = nil, e: Curve25519.KeyAgreement.PrivateKey? = nil) throws {
        self.pattern = pattern
        self.initiator = initiator
        self.s = s
        self.e = e
        self.remoteStatic = rs
        let name = Data(pattern.protocolName.utf8)
        h = name.count <= 32 ? name + Data(count: 32 - name.count) : LinkNoise.sha256(name)
        ck = h
        mixHash(prologue)
        if pattern.preMessages {
            // The pre-messages "-> s" then "<- s": the initiator's static key first, then the responder's.
            guard let rs, rs.count == 32 else { throw LinkNoiseError.needsRemoteStatic }
            let mine = LinkNoise.publicKey(of: s)
            mixHash(initiator ? mine : rs)
            mixHash(initiator ? rs : mine)
        }
    }

    var staticPublic: Data { LinkNoise.publicKey(of: s) }
    private var count: Int { pattern.messages.count }
    var isDone: Bool { index == count }
    var isMyTurn: Bool { index < count && (index % 2 == 0) == initiator }

    private mutating func mixHash(_ data: Data) { h = LinkNoise.sha256(h + data) }
    private mutating func mixKey(_ input: Data) {
        let (next, k) = LinkNoise.hkdf2(ck, input)
        ck = next
        cs = LinkCipherState(key: k)
    }
    private mutating func encryptAndHash(_ plain: Data) throws -> Data {
        let c = try cs.encrypt(ad: h, plain)
        mixHash(c)
        return c
    }
    private mutating func decryptAndHash(_ cipher: Data) throws -> Data {
        let p = try cs.decrypt(ad: h, cipher)
        mixHash(cipher)
        return p
    }

    private mutating func doDH(_ token: String) throws {
        let chars = Array(token)
        let (mine, theirs) = initiator ? (chars[0], chars[1]) : (chars[1], chars[0])
        let priv = mine == "e" ? e : s
        guard let priv, let pub = theirs == "e" ? re : remoteStatic else { throw LinkNoiseError.invalidKey }
        mixKey(try LinkNoise.dh(priv, pub))
    }

    mutating func write(_ payload: Data = Data()) throws -> Data {
        guard isMyTurn else { throw LinkNoiseError.notMyTurn }
        var out = Data()
        for token in pattern.messages[index] {
            switch token {
            case "e":
                if e == nil { e = Curve25519.KeyAgreement.PrivateKey() }
                let pub = LinkNoise.publicKey(of: e!)
                out += pub
                mixHash(pub)
            case "s":
                out += try encryptAndHash(LinkNoise.publicKey(of: s))
            default:
                try doDH(token)
            }
        }
        out += try encryptAndHash(payload)
        if out.count > LinkNoise.maxMessage { throw LinkNoiseError.tooLong }
        index += 1
        return out
    }

    mutating func read(_ message: Data) throws -> Data {
        guard !isDone, !isMyTurn else { throw LinkNoiseError.notMyTurn }
        if message.count > LinkNoise.maxMessage { throw LinkNoiseError.tooLong }
        let m = Data(message)   // re-based at 0, whatever slice it came from
        var pos = 0
        for token in pattern.messages[index] {
            switch token {
            case "e":
                guard m.count - pos >= 32 else { throw LinkNoiseError.short }
                let key = m.subdata(in: pos..<pos + 32)
                pos += 32
                re = key
                mixHash(key)
            case "s":
                let n = 32 + (cs.hasKey ? LinkNoise.tag : 0)
                guard m.count - pos >= n else { throw LinkNoiseError.short }
                remoteStatic = try decryptAndHash(m.subdata(in: pos..<pos + n))
                pos += n
            default:
                try doDH(token)
            }
        }
        let payload = try decryptAndHash(m.subdata(in: pos..<m.count))
        index += 1
        return payload
    }

    /// After the last message: the handshake hash, and the cipher states for this side (send, receive).
    func finish() throws -> (hash: Data, send: LinkCipherState, receive: LinkCipherState) {
        guard isDone else { throw LinkNoiseError.notFinished }
        let (k1, k2) = LinkNoise.hkdf2(ck, Data())
        let c1 = LinkCipherState(key: k1), c2 = LinkCipherState(key: k2)
        return initiator ? (h, c1, c2) : (h, c2, c1)
    }
}
````

### Task 2: The wire shapes and the file-name rules: frames, sanitizer

**Agent:** `nm-implementer-light` (Haiku 5.5, medium). The code is given in full; the fixtures prove it.

**Files:**
- Create: `NotchBuddy/Sources/App/LinkBytes.swift`, `LinkName.swift`, `LinkFrames.swift`; `tests/LinkNameTests.swift`, `tests/LinkFramesTests.swift`; `scripts/test-link-name.sh`, `scripts/test-link-frames.sh`
- Modify: `NotchBuddy/NotchBuddy.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` (regenerated)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `filename-cases.json` (`bytesMax`, `extBytesMax`, `collisionMax`, `cases[]` of `{name, raw, out}`, `uniqueCases[]`), `frames.json` (the numbers, `types`, `frames[]`, `rejects[]`, `limits[]`, `hello[]`, `txt[]`, `preamble[]`, `records[]`, `recordEncode[]`), `docs/LINK-CONTRACT.md` "File names" and "On the wire".
- Produces: `LinkBytes` (`hex`, `data(hex:)`, `isCanonicalHex`, `isHex32`, `isIdHex`, `zeroId`, big-endian readers), `LinkOwnId` (`.absent`, `.null`, `.value`), `LinkName` (`sanitize(_:) -> Result`, `uniqueName(_:taken:)`, `key`, `isInvisible`, `isBadCategory`, `isSpace`, `trim`, `isReserved`, `string`, `nfkc`), `LinkWire` (the constants, `encodeRecord`, `splitRecords`, `encodePreamble`/`decodePreamble`, `deviceName`, `encodeHello`/`decodeHello`, `parseTxt(_:ownId:)`, `Frame`, `encode`, `decode`, `planChunks`), `LinkFrameError`.

**Rules a port must not get wrong** (all in the code, all pinned): the sanitizer works on Unicode **scalars**, never on `Character`s (a Swift `Character` is a grapheme cluster, and `String ==` is canonical equivalence, so names are compared as scalar arrays or by their NFC key); `invisibleRanges` and the whitespace run are written out by hand, as in the reference; a name is cut at 200 UTF-8 bytes at a scalar, keeping an extension of up to 16 bytes; decoders are strict (`String(validating:as:)`, never a repairing decoder), refuse anything left over, and never trap on short input. The fixture holds a name that starts with a byte order mark: `JSONSerialization` drops it, so `LinkNameTests` reads `filename-cases.json` with `JSONDecoder`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests and their scripts**

The name program replays 162 cases and 14 collision cases and the invariants the reference fuzzes (6000 hostile names and 3000 names of 300 bytes or more). The frames program replays every frame (encode and decode), 37 damaged frames, the 65494/65495-byte chunk limit, 21 hellos, 23 adverts, the first records, the record cutter and an 8000-frame damage fuzz.

**Create `tests/LinkNameTests.swift`**

````swift
import Foundation

// The file-name rules of the link (docs/LINK-CONTRACT.md "File names"): filename-cases.json replayed against LinkName.swift, and the invariants the reference fuzzes.
// Run it through scripts/test-link-name.sh.

@main
enum LinkNameTests {
    /// A tiny seeded generator, so a failing fuzz can be replayed.
    struct Lcg { var s: UInt64; mutating func next(_ n: Int) -> Int { s = s &* 6364136223846793005 &+ 1442695040888963407; return Int((s >> 33) % UInt64(n)) } }

    /// A raw name as the fixture holds it: text, or anything else (null, a number, a boolean, a list, an object), which is not text.
    enum Raw: Decodable { case text(String), other
        init(from d: Decoder) throws { if let s = try? d.singleValueContainer().decode(String.self) { self = .text(s) } else { self = .other } }
    }
    struct Case: Decodable { let name: String; let raw: Raw; let out: [String: String] }
    struct Numbered: Decodable { let stem: String; let ext: String; let from: Int; let to: Int }
    struct Unique: Decodable { let name: String; let wanted: String; let taken: [String]; let takenNumbered: Numbered?; let out: String? }
    struct Fixture: Decodable { let bytesMax: Int; let extBytesMax: Int; let collisionMax: Int; let cases: [Case]; let uniqueCases: [Unique] }

    static func sanitize(_ raw: Raw) -> LinkName.Result {
        guard case .text(let s) = raw else { return .reject("not-text") }
        return LinkName.sanitize(s)
    }

    @MainActor static func main() {
        // JSONSerialization drops a byte order mark at the start of a string value, which the contract's "a byte order mark at the start" case needs: this file is read with JSONDecoder.
        let f = try! JSONDecoder().decode(Fixture.self, from: Data(contentsOf: CT.fixtureURL("filename-cases.json", dir: "link")))
        CT.section("the constants")
        CT.check("200 bytes, an extension of 16, 9999 collisions", [f.bytesMax, f.extBytesMax, f.collisionMax], [LinkName.bytesMax, LinkName.extBytesMax, LinkName.collisionMax])

        CT.section("filename-cases.json: cases")
        var diffs: [String] = []
        for c in f.cases {
            let got = sanitize(c.raw)
            let want: LinkName.Result = c.out["ok"].map { .ok($0) } ?? .reject(c.out["reject"]!)
            // Compared as scalars: Swift's own String == is canonical equivalence, and the contract keeps NFC and NFKC apart.
            func same(_ a: LinkName.Result, _ b: LinkName.Result) -> Bool {
                switch (a, b) {
                case (.ok(let x), .ok(let y)): return Array(x.unicodeScalars) == Array(y.unicodeScalars)
                case (.reject(let x), .reject(let y)): return x == y
                default: return false
                }
            }
            if !same(got, want) { diffs.append("\(c.name): got \(got), want \(want)") }
        }
        CT.check("all \(f.cases.count) cases give the saved name or the reason the fixture says", diffs, [])

        CT.section("filename-cases.json: uniqueCases")
        diffs = []
        for c in f.uniqueCases {
            var taken = c.taken
            if let t = c.takenNumbered { for n in t.from...t.to { taken.append("\(t.stem) (\(n))\(t.ext)") } }
            let got = LinkName.uniqueName(c.wanted, taken: taken)
            if got.map({ Array($0.unicodeScalars) }) != c.out.map({ Array($0.unicodeScalars) }) { diffs.append("\(c.name): got \(String(describing: got)), want \(String(describing: c.out))") }
        }
        CT.check("all \(f.uniqueCases.count) collision cases", diffs, [])

        CT.section("what the fixtures cannot write")
        func ok(_ raw: String) -> String? { if case .ok(let n) = LinkName.sanitize(raw) { return n }; return nil }
        func rej(_ raw: String) -> String? { if case .reject(let r) = LinkName.sanitize(raw) { return r }; return nil }
        CT.check("a run of 250 spaces is one space before the cut: CON, 250 spaces, x.txt is CON x.txt", ok("CON" + String(repeating: " ", count: 250) + "x.txt"), "CON x.txt")
        CT.check("a run of 250 spaces after a dot is one space, and the leading-dot rule gives _. x", ok("." + String(repeating: " ", count: 250) + "x"), "_. x")
        CT.check("the cut leaves dots and spaces only: refused as empty", rej(String(repeating: ". ", count: 120) + String(repeating: "x", count: 20)), "empty")
        CT.check("the cut leaves full-width dots and spaces only, which NFKC makes dots: refused as empty", rej(String(repeating: "\u{FF0E} ", count: 120) + String(repeating: "x", count: 20)), "empty")
        CT.check("a Swift String compares by canonical equivalence, so the key is the NFC scalars case-folded", [LinkName.key("e\u{301}"), LinkName.key("\u{E9}")].map { Array($0.unicodeScalars) }, [Array("\u{E9}".unicodeScalars), Array("\u{E9}".unicodeScalars)])
        CT.check("ß folds to ss (a documented limit: the second of straße.txt and strasse.txt is numbered)", LinkName.uniqueName("strasse.txt", taken: ["stra\u{DF}e.txt"]), "strasse (1).txt")
        CT.check("a leading dot is not an extension", LinkName.uniqueName(".bashrc", taken: [".bashrc"]), ".bashrc (1)")

        CT.section("6000 hostile names: what is accepted is a safe name, and cleaning it again changes nothing")
        var rnd = Lcg(s: 20261014)
        let alphabet: [String] = Array("ab.. /\\:*?\"<>|~%$").map(String.init) + ["\u{0}", "\u{1f}", "\u{7f}", "\u{85}", "\u{200b}", "\u{200d}", "\u{202e}", "\u{2066}", "\u{feff}", "\u{2028}", "\u{a0}", "\u{3000}", "\u{FF0E}", "\u{FF0F}", "\u{FF3C}", "e\u{301}", "\u{E9}", "\u{1F408}", "\u{65E5}", "CON", "nul", "COM1", "\u{B9}", "\u{FB01}"]
        let tails = ["", ".txt", ".", " ", ".tar.gz"]
        var bad: String?
        for _ in 0..<6000 {
            var raw = ""
            for _ in 0..<rnd.next(40) { raw += alphabet[rnd.next(alphabet.count)] }
            raw += tails[rnd.next(tails.count)]
            guard case .ok(let name) = LinkName.sanitize(raw) else {
                if case .reject(let r) = LinkName.sanitize(raw), !["invisible", "separator", "empty"].contains(r) { bad = "reason \(r) for \(raw.unicodeScalars.map(\.value))"; break }
                continue
            }
            let sc = Array(name.unicodeScalars), nk = LinkName.nfkc(sc)
            let stem = LinkName.string(Array(sc.prefix { $0 != "." }))
            let reservedStem = LinkName.isReserved(sc)
            let unique = LinkName.uniqueName(name, taken: [name])
            let checks: [Bool] = [
                !sc.isEmpty, name.utf8.count <= LinkName.bytesMax, Array(name.precomposedStringWithCanonicalMapping.unicodeScalars) == sc,
                !(sc + nk).contains { $0 == "/" || $0 == "\\" }, !sc.contains { "<>:\"|?*".unicodeScalars.contains($0) }, !sc.contains { LinkName.isBadCategory($0) },
                sc.last != "." && sc.last != " ", sc.first != ".", !LinkName.isSpace(sc[0]), !LinkName.trim(nk).isEmpty, !reservedStem || sc.first == "_" || stem.isEmpty,
                { if case .ok(let again) = LinkName.sanitize(name) { return Array(again.unicodeScalars) == sc }; return false }(),
                unique != nil && unique!.utf8.count <= LinkName.bytesMax + 7,
            ]
            if checks.contains(false) { bad = "\(raw.unicodeScalars.map(\.value)) -> \(name) \(checks)"; break }
        }
        CT.check("no hostile name gets through", bad, nil)

        CT.section("3000 raw names of 300 bytes or more: at most 200 bytes, a device name never without its _")
        let heads = ["CON", "nul", "COM1", "LPT9", "aux", "CONOUT$", "\u{FF23}\u{FF2F}\u{FF2E}", "COM\u{B9}"]
        let pieces = [" ", " ", " ", " ", " ", " ", "a", "x", "Z", ".", "\u{E9}", "\u{1F408}", "\u{65E5}", "\u{301}"]
        let ends = ["", ".txt", ".tar.gz", ".bin", " .log"]
        var longBad: String?
        for _ in 0..<3000 {
            var raw = heads[rnd.next(heads.count)] + String(repeating: " ", count: 150 + rnd.next(150))
            let target = 300 + rnd.next(60)
            while raw.utf8.count < target { raw += pieces[rnd.next(pieces.count)] }
            raw += ends[rnd.next(ends.count)]
            guard case .ok(let name) = LinkName.sanitize(raw) else { continue }
            let sc = Array(name.unicodeScalars)
            if name.utf8.count > LinkName.bytesMax || ((LinkName.isReserved(sc) || LinkName.isReserved(LinkName.nfkc(sc))) && sc.first != "_") { longBad = "\(raw.prefix(20)) -> \(name)"; break }
        }
        CT.check("no long name breaks the limit or hides a device name", longBad, nil)
        CT.finish()
    }
}
````

**Create `tests/LinkFramesTests.swift`**

````swift
import Foundation

// The wire shapes of the link (docs/LINK-CONTRACT.md "On the wire"): frames.json replayed against LinkFrames.swift, and a seeded fuzz of damaged frames.
// Run it through scripts/test-link-frames.sh.

@main
enum LinkFramesTests {
    typealias W = LinkWire

    static func bytes(_ hex: String) -> Data { LinkBytes.data(hex: hex)! }
    static func u64(_ v: Any?) -> UInt64 { (v as! NSNumber).uint64Value }

    /// A fixture frame (a dictionary) as the typed frame.
    static func frame(_ d: [String: Any]) -> W.Frame {
        let type = d["type"] as! String
        if type == "pair-yes" { return .pairYes }
        if type == "pair-no" { return .pairNo }
        let id = bytes(d["id"] as! String)
        switch type {
        case "offer": return .offer(id: id, size: u64(d["size"]), name: d["name"] as! String, kind: d["kind"] as! String)
        case "accept": return .accept(id: id)
        case "decline": return .decline(id: id, reason: UInt8(d["reason"] as! Int))
        case "chunk": return .chunk(id: id, offset: u64(d["offset"]), data: bytes(d["data"] as! String))
        case "done": return .done(id: id)
        default: return .cancel(id: id)
        }
    }

    /// The error code of a failed call, or nil.
    static func code<T>(_ f: () throws -> T) -> String? {
        do { _ = try f(); return nil } catch { return (error as? LinkFrameError)?.rawValue ?? "other:\(error)" }
    }

    struct Lcg { var s: UInt64; mutating func next(_ n: Int) -> Int { s = s &* 6364136223846793005 &+ 1442695040888963407; return Int((s >> 33) % UInt64(n)) } }

    @MainActor static func main() {
        let f = CT.fixture("frames.json", dir: "link")
        CT.section("the constants")
        CT.check("the numbers the rules name", [f["noiseMax"] as! Int, f["tag"] as! Int, f["idLen"] as! Int, f["protocolVersion"] as! Int, f["chunkMax"] as! Int, f["chunkSize"] as! Int, f["rawNameMax"] as! Int, f["kindMax"] as! Int, f["deviceNameMax"] as! Int],
                 [W.noiseMax, W.tag, W.idLen, Int(W.protocolVersion), W.chunkMax, W.chunkSize, W.rawNameMax, W.kindMax, W.deviceNameMax])
        CT.check("4 GiB inclusive", u64(f["sizeMax"]), W.sizeMax)
        CT.check("the type numbers", (f["types"] as! [String: Int]).sorted { $0.key < $1.key }.map(\.value), ["accept": 2, "cancel": 6, "chunk": 4, "decline": 3, "done": 5, "offer": 1, "pairNo": 34, "pairYes": 33].sorted { $0.key < $1.key }.map(\.value))
        CT.check("a chunk frame at its largest plus the tag fills one Noise message exactly", 1 + W.idLen + 8 + W.chunkMax + W.tag, W.noiseMax)

        CT.section("frames: encode and decode")
        var diffs: [String] = []
        for c in f["frames"] as! [[String: Any]] {
            let want = frame(c["frame"] as! [String: Any]), hex = c["hex"] as! String
            if (try? W.encode(want)).map(LinkBytes.hex) != hex { diffs.append("encode: \(c["name"] as! String)") }
            if (try? W.decode(bytes(hex))) != want { diffs.append("decode: \(c["name"] as! String)") }
        }
        CT.check("every frame encodes to its hex and decodes back", diffs, [])

        CT.section("rejects and limits")
        diffs = []
        for c in f["rejects"] as! [[String: Any]] where code({ try W.decode(bytes(c["hex"] as! String)) }) != (c["error"] as! String) { diffs.append(c["name"] as! String) }
        CT.check("every damaged frame is refused with the fixture's cause", diffs, [])
        diffs = []
        for c in f["limits"] as! [[String: Any]] {
            let fill = bytes(c["fill"] as! String), count = c["count"] as! Int
            var data = Data(); for _ in 0..<count { data.append(fill) }
            let raw = Data([4]) + bytes(c["id"] as! String) + Data(W_be(u64(c["offset"]))) + data
            if (code({ try W.decode(raw) }) ?? "ok") != (c["out"] as! String) { diffs.append(c["name"] as! String) }
        }
        CT.check("a chunk of 65494 bytes is good, 65495 is chunk-long", diffs, [])

        CT.section("hello, advert, first record, records")
        diffs = []
        for c in f["hello"] as! [[String: Any]] {
            let out = c["out"] as! [String: String], hex = c["hex"] as! String
            if let e = out["error"] {
                if code({ try W.decodeHello(bytes(hex)) }) != e { diffs.append("hello: \(c["name"] as! String)") }
            } else if let h = try? W.decodeHello(bytes(hex)) {
                if h != W.Hello(id: out["id"]!, nonce: out["nonce"]!, name: out["name"]!) { diffs.append("hello: \(c["name"] as! String)") }
                if LinkBytes.hex(W.encodeHello(deviceId: bytes(out["id"]!), nonce: bytes(out["nonce"]!), name: out["name"]!)) != hex { diffs.append("hello re-encoded: \(c["name"] as! String)") }
            } else { diffs.append("hello: \(c["name"] as! String)") }
        }
        for c in f["txt"] as! [[String: Any]] {
            let out = c["out"] as! [String: String]
            guard let rec = c["txt"] as? [String: Any] else {
                if out["error"] != "not-a-record" { diffs.append("advert: \(c["name"] as! String)") }
                continue   // a list and null are not records: the typed call cannot be given one
            }
            var own = LinkOwnId.absent
            if c["ownId"] != nil { own = (c["ownId"] as? String).map { .value($0) } ?? .null }
            let txt = rec.compactMapValues { $0 as? String }      // a number is no text: the version check then sees nothing
            let got: [String: String]
            do { let a = try W.parseTxt(txt, ownId: own); got = ["id": a.id, "name": a.name] } catch { got = ["error": (error as? LinkFrameError)?.rawValue ?? "other"] }
            if got != out { diffs.append("advert: \(c["name"] as! String): \(got)") }
        }
        for c in f["preamble"] as! [[String: Any]] {
            let out = c["out"] as! [String: String], hex = c["hex"] as! String
            if let e = out["error"] { if code({ try W.decodePreamble(bytes(hex)) }) != e { diffs.append("preamble: \(c["name"] as! String)") }; continue }
            let p = try? W.decodePreamble(bytes(hex))
            if p?.id != out["id"] || (p?.mode == .pair) != (out["mode"] == "pair") || p.map({ LinkBytes.hex(W.encodePreamble(mode: $0.mode, deviceId: bytes($0.id))) }) != hex { diffs.append("preamble: \(c["name"] as! String)") }
        }
        for c in f["records"] as! [[String: Any]] {
            let out = c["out"] as! [String: Any], hex = c["hex"] as! String
            do {
                let r = try W.splitRecords(bytes(hex))
                if r.records.map(LinkBytes.hex) != (out["records"] as! [String]) || LinkBytes.hex(r.rest) != (out["rest"] as! String) { diffs.append("records: \(c["name"] as! String)") }
            } catch { if (error as? LinkFrameError)?.rawValue != (out["error"] as? String) { diffs.append("records: \(c["name"] as! String)") } }
        }
        for c in f["recordEncode"] as! [[String: Any]] {
            let out = c["out"] as! [String: String]
            do { if LinkBytes.hex(try W.encodeRecord(bytes(c["payload"] as! String))) != out["hex"] { diffs.append("record: \(c["name"] as! String)") } }
            catch { if (error as? LinkFrameError)?.rawValue != out["error"] { diffs.append("record: \(c["name"] as! String)") } }
        }
        CT.check("every hello, advert, first record and record case is as the fixture says", diffs, [])
        CT.check("a record of 65535 bytes is made, one of 65536 is not", [(try? W.encodeRecord(Data(count: 65535)))?.count ?? -1, code { try W.encodeRecord(Data(count: 65536)) } == "record-length" ? 1 : 0], [65537, 1])
        CT.check("the nonce of an advert's own id given as null refuses every advert", code { try W.parseTxt(["v": "1", "id": "ab" + String(repeating: "cd", count: 15), "name": "Mac"], ownId: .null) }, "device-id")

        CT.section("planChunks")
        CT.check("an empty file has none; 10 bytes by 4 are 4, 4, 2; a multiple fits exactly", [W.planChunks(size: 0).count, W.planChunks(size: 10, chunkSize: 4).map(\.length).reduce(0, +), W.planChunks(size: 65536).count], [0, 10, 2])
        CT.check("the offsets are contiguous", W.planChunks(size: 10, chunkSize: 4).map { Int($0.offset) }, [0, 4, 8])

        CT.section("8000 damaged frames: nothing but a LinkFrameError, and what is accepted encodes back to the same bytes")
        var rnd = Lcg(s: 20261014)
        let seeds = (f["frames"] as! [[String: Any]]).map { bytes($0["hex"] as! String) }.filter { $0.count < 200 }
        var bad: String?
        for _ in 0..<8000 {
            var b = [UInt8](seeds[rnd.next(seeds.count)])
            for _ in 0..<rnd.next(4) {
                switch rnd.next(4) {
                case 0 where !b.isEmpty: b[rnd.next(b.count)] = UInt8(rnd.next(256))
                case 1 where !b.isEmpty: b.removeSubrange(rnd.next(b.count)...)
                case 2: for _ in 0..<(1 + rnd.next(3)) { b.append(UInt8(rnd.next(256))) }
                default: if !b.isEmpty { b.insert(UInt8(rnd.next(256)), at: rnd.next(b.count)) }
                }
            }
            do { let fr = try W.decode(Data(b)); if try W.encode(fr) != Data(b) { bad = LinkBytes.hex(b); break } }
            catch is LinkFrameError {} catch { bad = "other error \(error)"; break }
        }
        CT.check("no damaged frame traps or re-encodes differently", bad, nil)
        CT.finish()
    }

    static func W_be(_ v: UInt64) -> [UInt8] { LinkBytes.beUInt64(v) }
}
````
