- [ ] **Step 4: Write the service and the views**

**Create `NotchBuddy/Sources/App/LinkService.swift`**

````swift
import Combine
import CryptoKit
import Foundation
@preconcurrency import Network

// The app's one holder of the link (sub-project G2): the settings, the listener and the browser, the pairing and the transfers that are under way, and what the views show. It is the only file
// that joins the pure rules (LinkPairing, LinkFlow), the conversations (LinkPairRun, LinkTransferReceive, LinkTransferSend), the Keychain (LinkKeychain) and the network (LinkNetwork) to the
// app. Views take plain values (`LinkService.Card`, `LinkDirectory.Row`) and closures, so the render script draws them without this file.
//
// What it will not do: it starts no listener and makes no advert until the user turns on "Visible on this network" (off by default), it browses only while Send or Devices is open, it never
// accepts an offer for the user (`respondOffer(accept:)` is only ever called from a click), it keeps no file name, device name or address in a log, and it polls nothing: every change comes from
// a socket, a callback or a click. It starts no timer of its own. The 2-minute windows are one-shot sleeps inside the conversations, started when a pairing or an offer begins.

@MainActor
final class LinkService: ObservableObject {
    static let shared = LinkService()
    /// In every build (Debug, Release and App Store): `scripts/check-app-binary.sh` looks for it. The Devices section carries it as its accessibility identifier. A marker must be 16 bytes or more:
    /// Swift keeps a shorter string literal inside the instruction that uses it, where `strings` cannot see it, and a Release build would then seem to lack the link.
    static let marker = "NM_LINK_ENGINE_ACTIVE"

    /// What the `.link` island view shows.
    enum Card: Equatable {
        /// A pairing under way: the six digits, the other machine's authenticated name, and whether this user has answered.
        case pair(code: String, peer: String, answered: Bool)
        /// An offer waiting for a click. Nothing accepts it but the user.
        case offer(LinkOffer)
        case receiving(LinkOffer, received: UInt64)
        /// The end of something: a short title and line, and the file when one was saved.
        case result(title: String, detail: String, file: URL?)
    }

    @Published private(set) var settings: LinkSettings
    @Published private(set) var rows: [LinkDirectory.Row] = []
    @Published private(set) var listening = false
    /// A sentence for Settings → Devices when the listener or the Keychain could not do what was asked; nil otherwise.
    @Published private(set) var problem: String?
    @Published private(set) var card: Card? { didSet { cardChanged(old: oldValue) } }
    /// One line for the shelf's bar while this Mac sends ("Sending a.pdf to Desk PC, 45 %"). No file or machine name goes to a log.
    @Published private(set) var sendStatus: String?
    /// A one-line note for Devices after a pairing that did not happen ("Wait 30 seconds before trying again").
    @Published private(set) var note: String?

    private let backing: any LinkSettingsBacking
    private let keychain: LinkKeychain
    private let folder: URL
    private let host: String
    private var listener: LinkListener?
    private var browser: LinkBrowser?
    private var browsers = 0
    private var seen: [String: LinkBrowser.Found] = [:]
    #if DEBUG && !APPSTORE
    /// Debug builds only: machines the Debug fake peer (LinkDevPeer.swift) put on the list, so a pairing and a transfer can be tried on the loopback address without a second computer.
    private var injected: [String: LinkBrowser.Found] = [:]
    private(set) var listenerPort: UInt16?
    private var found: [String: LinkBrowser.Found] { seen.merging(injected) { a, _ in a } }
    func debugInject(_ list: [LinkBrowser.Found]) { injected = Dictionary(list.map { ($0.advert.id, $0) }, uniquingKeysWith: { a, _ in a }); refreshRows() }
    func debugReceivedFolder() -> URL { folder }
    #else
    private var found: [String: LinkBrowser.Found] { seen }
    #endif
    private var pairingState = (initiator: LinkPairing.InitiatorState(), responder: LinkPairing.ResponderState())
    private var pairRun: LinkPairRun?
    /// Who may start a pairing with this Mac: only while Devices is open, or for 2 minutes after the user clicked Pair here (`LinkPairing.Gate`).
    private var pairGate = LinkPairing.Gate()
    private var pairingIncoming = false
    private var receiveRun: LinkTransferReceive?
    private var sendRun: LinkTransferSend?
    private var sendQueueCancelled = false
    private let busy = Busy()
    private var peers: [String: LinkPeer] = [:]

    init(backing: any LinkSettingsBacking = UserDefaults.standard, keychain: LinkKeychain = LinkKeychain(), folder: URL = LinkFileSink.defaultFolder(), host: String = Host.current().localizedName ?? "Mac") {
        self.backing = backing
        self.keychain = keychain
        self.folder = folder
        self.host = host
        settings = LinkSettings.load(from: backing, host: host)
    }

    private final class Busy: @unchecked Sendable {
        private let lock = NSLock(); private var peers: Set<String> = []
        func claim(_ id: String) -> Bool { lock.withLock { peers.insert(id).inserted } }
        func release(_ id: String) { lock.withLock { _ = peers.remove(id) } }
    }

    // MARK: Start, stop, settings

    /// At launch: reads the paired machines and, only when the user has turned "Visible on this network" on, starts the listener. Costs nothing otherwise.
    func start() {
        peers = keychain.peers()
        refreshRows()
        if settings.visible { startListening() }
    }

    func setVisible(_ on: Bool) {
        guard on != settings.visible else { return }
        settings.visible = on
        settings.save(to: backing)
        if on { startListening() } else { stopListening() }
        refreshRows()
    }

    /// False when the name is not one a card would accept.
    @discardableResult
    func setDeviceName(_ name: String) -> Bool {
        guard let valid = LinkSettings.validName(name) else { return false }
        settings.deviceName = valid
        settings.save(to: backing)
        if settings.visible { stopListening(); startListening() }   // the advert carries the name
        return true
    }

    private func identity() -> LinkIdentity? {
        do { return LinkIdentity(deviceId: try keychain.deviceId(), name: settings.deviceName, key: try keychain.staticKey()) }
        catch { problem = "The link's key could not be read from the Keychain."; return nil }
    }

    private func startListening() {
        guard listener == nil, let me = identity() else { return }
        let l = LinkListener(onConnection: { [weak self] transport, slot in Task { @MainActor in await self?.handleIncoming(transport, slot) } },
                             onState: { [weak self] port, failed in Task { @MainActor in self?.listenerState(port: port, failed: failed) } })
        do { try l.start(advert: (me.deviceId, me.name)); listener = l; problem = nil; appendAppLog("link.log", "listening") }
        catch { problem = "This Mac could not start listening."; listening = false }
    }

    private func stopListening() {
        listener?.stop(); listener = nil
        listening = false
        pairRun?.cancel()
        receiveRun?.cancel()
        sendRun?.cancel()
        appendAppLog("link.log", "stopped listening")
    }

    private func recordPort(_ port: UInt16?) {
        #if DEBUG && !APPSTORE
        listenerPort = port
        #endif
    }

    private func listenerState(port: UInt16?, failed: Bool) {
        if failed { listening = false; problem = "This Mac could not listen. Check System Settings, Privacy & Security, Local Network." }
        else if port != nil { listening = true; problem = nil; recordPort(port) }
    }

    // MARK: Browsing (only while Send or Devices is open)

    func beginBrowsing() {
        browsers += 1
        guard browser == nil, settings.visible, let id = try? keychain.deviceId() else { return }
        let b = LinkBrowser(ownId: id) { [weak self] results in Task { @MainActor in self?.browsed(results) } }
        browser = b
        b.start()
    }

    func endBrowsing() {
        browsers = max(0, browsers - 1)
        guard browsers == 0 else { return }
        browser?.stop(); browser = nil
        seen = [:]
        refreshRows()
    }

    private func browsed(_ results: [LinkBrowser.Found]) {
        seen = Dictionary(results.map { ($0.advert.id, $0) }, uniquingKeysWith: { a, _ in a })
        refreshRows()
    }

    private func refreshRows() {
        peers = keychain.peers()
        let own = (try? keychain.deviceId()) ?? ""
        rows = LinkDirectory.rows(adverts: found.values.map(\.advert), peers: peers, ownId: own)
    }

    func forget(_ id: String) { keychain.forget(id); refreshRows() }

    /// Settings → Devices is on screen (or not): while it is, another machine may start a pairing with this one.
    func devicesOpened() { pairGate.devicesAppeared() }
    func devicesClosed() { pairGate.devicesDisappeared() }

    // MARK: Pairing

    /// "Pair a machine": this Mac starts a pairing with a machine it has seen.
    func pair(with id: String) {
        guard settings.visible else { note = "Turn on Visible on this network first."; return }
        guard pairRun == nil, let target = found[id], let me = identity() else { return }
        pairGate.pairClicked(now: Self.nowMs())
        note = nil
        let nonce = Data((0..<32).map { _ in UInt8.random(in: 0...255) })
        Task {
            do {
                let transport = try await LinkNWTransport.ready(NWConnection(to: target.endpoint, using: .tcp), on: DispatchQueue(label: "notchmeow.link.out"))
                let run = LinkPairRun(transport: transport, identity: me, nonce: nonce, clock: Self.nowMs, hooks: self.pairHooks())
                self.pairRun = run
                appendAppLog("link.log", "a pairing started")
                var state = self.pairingState.initiator
                state.ids = self.pairIds(me)
                let (outcome, next) = await run.runInitiator(state)
                self.pairingState.initiator = next
                self.pairEnded(outcome, ownId: me.deviceId)
            } catch { self.pairRun = nil; self.note = "Could not reach that machine." }
        }
    }

    private func pairIds(_ me: LinkIdentity) -> LinkPairing.Ids { LinkPairing.Ids(ownId: .value(me.deviceId), paired: keychain.pairedKeys()) }

    private nonisolated func pairHooks(done: @escaping @Sendable () -> Void = {}) -> LinkPairRun.Hooks {
        LinkPairRun.Hooks(showCode: { [weak self] code, name in Task { @MainActor in self?.card = .pair(code: code, peer: name, answered: false) } }, handshakeDone: done)
    }

    func answerPair(yes: Bool) {
        guard case .pair(let code, let peer, false)? = card else { return }
        card = .pair(code: code, peer: peer, answered: true)
        pairRun?.answer(yes)
    }

    private func pairEnded(_ outcome: LinkPairOutcome, ownId: String) {
        pairRun = nil
        appendAppLog("link.log", "a pairing ended")
        switch outcome {
        case .paired(let peer):
            // Both users said yes. The paired map is read again here, at the moment of storing (two pairings can overlap for up to two minutes).
            do { try keychain.add(peer, ownId: ownId); card = .result(title: "Paired", detail: "This Mac and \(peer.name) can now send files to each other.", file: nil) }
            catch { card = .result(title: "Not paired", detail: "That machine could not be saved.", file: nil) }
        case .declined(let byMe): card = byMe ? nil : .result(title: "Not paired", detail: "The other machine said the codes did not match.", file: nil)
        case .refused(let why): card = nil; note = why == LinkPairing.Refusal.attemptTooSoon.rawValue ? "Wait 30 seconds before trying again." : "The pairing did not go through."
        }
        refreshRows()
    }

    // MARK: Incoming connections

    private func handleIncoming(_ t: LinkNWTransport, _ slot: LinkPendingSlot) async {
        guard let me = identity(), let pre = try? LinkWire.decodePreamble(try await t.receive()) else { slot.finish(); await t.close(); return }
        switch pre.mode {
        case .pair:
            // Outside the gate (Devices closed and no Pair click in the last 2 minutes) or while another pairing runs: refused silently, nothing sent, no card, no alert.
            guard pairRun == nil, !pairingIncoming, pairGate.allows(now: Self.nowMs()) else { slot.finish(); await t.close(); return }
            pairingIncoming = true
            var state = pairingState.responder
            state.ids = pairIds(me)
            let result = await LinkIncomingPairing.run(transport: t, identity: me, nonce: Data((0..<32).map { _ in UInt8.random(in: 0...255) }), clock: Self.nowMs, gate: pairGate, state: state,
                                                       hooks: pairHooks(done: { slot.finish() }), made: { [weak self] run in Task { @MainActor in self?.pairRun = run } })
            pairingIncoming = false
            slot.finish()
            guard let (outcome, next) = result else { return }
            appendAppLog("link.log", "a pairing started")
            pairingState.responder = next
            pairEnded(outcome, ownId: me.deviceId)
        case .transfer:
            // A transfer runs only between paired machines: an id this Mac does not know is closed without an answer.
            guard receiveRun == nil, let peer = keychain.peer(pre.id) else { slot.finish(); await t.close(); return }
            let busy = self.busy
            let run = LinkTransferReceive(transport: t, identity: me, peer: peer, folder: folder, hooks: LinkTransferReceive.Hooks(
                offered: { [weak self] offer in Task { @MainActor in self?.card = .offer(offer) } },
                progress: { [weak self] got, _ in Task { @MainActor in if case .offer(let o)? = self?.card { self?.card = .receiving(o, received: got) } else if case .receiving(let o, _)? = self?.card { self?.card = .receiving(o, received: got) } } },
                claim: { busy.claim($0) }, release: { busy.release($0) }, handshakeDone: { slot.finish() }))
            receiveRun = run
            appendAppLog("link.log", "a transfer started")
            let outcome = await run.run()
            receiveRun = nil
            appendAppLog("link.log", "a transfer ended")
            receiveEnded(outcome, peer: peer.name)
        }
    }

    /// The user's click on Accept or Decline. The only way an offer is accepted.
    func respondOffer(accept: Bool) {
        guard case .offer(let o)? = card else { return }
        if accept { card = .receiving(o, received: 0) }
        receiveRun?.respond(accept: accept)
    }

    func cancelReceiving() { receiveRun?.cancel() }

    private func receiveEnded(_ outcome: LinkTransferOutcome, peer: String) {
        switch outcome {
        case .completed(let url): card = .result(title: "Received", detail: url.map { "Saved as \($0.lastPathComponent) in Downloads/NotchMeow." } ?? "Saved.", file: url)
        case .declined, .withdrawn: card = nil
        case .expired: card = .result(title: "Offer expired", detail: "Nobody answered in 2 minutes, so it was declined.", file: nil)
        case .cancelled: card = .result(title: "Transfer cancelled", detail: "Nothing was saved.", file: nil)
        case .aborted: card = .result(title: "Transfer stopped", detail: "The connection ended before the file was complete. Nothing was saved.", file: nil)
        }
    }

    func dismissCard() { if case .offer? = card { respondOffer(accept: false) } else { card = nil } }

    // MARK: Sending

    /// Sends the shelf's items to a paired machine, one after the other (each is its own offer, and the other user answers each).
    func send(_ items: [ShelfItem], to id: String, model: ShelfModel) {
        guard sendRun == nil, let peer = keychain.peer(id), let target = found[id], let me = identity() else { sendStatus = "That machine is not on the network."; return }
        sendQueueCancelled = false
        let accesses = items.compactMap { model.open($0) }
        guard accesses.count == items.count else { sendStatus = "A file is missing."; accesses.forEach { $0.end() }; return }
        Task {
            defer { accesses.forEach { $0.end() } }
            var sent = 0
            for access in accesses {
                if self.sendQueueCancelled { break }
                let source: LinkFileSource
                do { source = try LinkFileSource(url: access.url) } catch { self.sendStatus = "\(access.url.lastPathComponent) cannot be sent (a folder, a file over 4 GB, or a name that is too long)."; continue }
                self.sendStatus = "Waiting for \(peer.name) to accept \(source.name)"
                do {
                    let transport = try await LinkNWTransport.ready(NWConnection(to: target.endpoint, using: .tcp), on: DispatchQueue(label: "notchmeow.link.out"))
                    let run = LinkTransferSend(transport: transport, identity: me, peer: peer, source: source, hooks: .init(progress: { done, total in
                        Task { @MainActor in self.sendStatus = "Sending \(source.name) to \(peer.name), \(LinkText.percent(done, total)) %" }
                    }))
                    self.sendRun = run
                    appendAppLog("link.log", "a send started")
                    let outcome = await run.run()
                    self.sendRun = nil
                    switch outcome {
                    case .completed: sent += 1; self.sendStatus = "Sent \(source.name) to \(peer.name)"
                    case .declined(let r): self.sendStatus = r == 1 ? "\(peer.name) did not answer in time" : r == 2 ? "\(peer.name) is receiving something else right now" : r == 3 ? "\(source.name) is too large" : "\(peer.name) declined \(source.name)"
                    case .expired, .withdrawn, .cancelled: self.sendStatus = "Cancelled"; self.sendQueueCancelled = true
                    case .aborted: self.sendStatus = "The connection to \(peer.name) ended"
                    }
                } catch { self.sendStatus = "Could not reach \(peer.name)." }
            }
            if sent > 1 { self.sendStatus = "Sent \(sent) files to \(peer.name)" }
        }
    }

    func cancelSending() { sendQueueCancelled = true; sendRun?.cancel() }

    // MARK: Island

    private func cardChanged(old: Card?) {
        let state = AppState.shared
        let offer: Bool = { if case .offer? = card { return true }; return false }()
        let hold: Bool = { switch card { case .pair?, .offer?, .receiving?: return true; default: return false } }()
        if state.linkOfferPending != offer { state.linkOfferPending = offer }
        if state.linkHoldsIsland != hold { state.linkHoldsIsland = hold }
        if card != nil && old == nil || (card != nil && !sameKind(card, old)) { NotificationCenter.default.post(name: .hookExpand, object: IslandView.link) }
        if card == nil, state.view == .link { NotificationCenter.default.post(name: .islandCollapse, object: nil) }
    }

    private func sameKind(_ a: Card?, _ b: Card?) -> Bool {
        switch (a, b) {
        case (.pair?, .pair?), (.offer?, .offer?), (.receiving?, .receiving?), (.result?, .result?): return true
        default: return false
        }
    }

    nonisolated static func nowMs() -> Int { Int(ProcessInfo.processInfo.systemUptime * 1000) }
}
````

**Create `NotchBuddy/Sources/App/LinkViews.swift`**

````swift
import SwiftUI

// The link's views (sub-project G2), in the style the app already has: the island card (`CardBackground`, the 12.5 pt buttons, grey 11 pt notes) for the pairing, the Accept card and the progress, and
// the GroupBox rows Settings already uses for Settings → Devices. They take plain values and closures, so scripts/render-link.sh draws them without the app. Nothing here changes a view that ships.

/// The size of a file the way people say it ("2.4 MB"): 1000-based, as Finder does.
enum LinkText {
    static func size(_ bytes: UInt64) -> String { ByteCountFormatter.string(fromByteCount: Int64(min(bytes, UInt64(Int64.max))), countStyle: .file) }
    /// "483 291": the six digits in two groups, to read out and compare.
    static func spaced(_ code: String) -> String { code.count == 6 ? "\(code.prefix(3)) \(code.suffix(3))" : code }
    static func percent(_ done: UInt64, _ total: UInt64) -> Int { total == 0 ? 100 : Int(min(100, done * 100 / total)) }
}

/// What the `.link` card's buttons do.
struct LinkCardActions {
    var answerPair: (Bool) -> Void = { _ in }
    var respondOffer: (Bool) -> Void = { _ in }
    var cancel: () -> Void = {}
    var showInFinder: (URL) -> Void = { _ in }
    var dismiss: () -> Void = {}
}

/// The `.link` island view's content: the pairing card, the Accept card, the progress of a file that is coming in, and the end of any of them.
struct LinkCardView: View {
    let card: LinkService.Card?
    var actions = LinkCardActions()

    var body: some View {
        ZStack {
            switch card {
            case .pair(let code, let peer, let answered)?: pair(code: code, peer: peer, answered: answered)
            case .offer(let o)?: offer(o)
            case .receiving(let o, let got)?: receiving(o, got)
            case .result(let title, let detail, let file)?: result(title, detail, file)
            case nil: CardBackground(wash: nil)
            }
        }
    }

    private func pad<V: View>(_ v: V) -> some View {
        v.padding(.leading, 116).padding(.trailing, 16).padding(.vertical, 4).frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .leading)
    }

    private func pair(code: String, peer: String, answered: Bool) -> some View {
        ZStack {
            CardBackground(wash: .indigo)
            pad(VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 5) {
                Text("Pair with \(peer)?").font(.system(size: 12, weight: .semibold)).foregroundColor(Color(hex: "#F5F6F8")).lineLimit(1).truncationMode(.tail)
                HStack(spacing: 10) {
                    Text(LinkText.spaced(code)).font(.system(size: 22, weight: .semibold, design: .monospaced)).foregroundColor(Color(hex: "#F5F6F8")).accessibilityLabel("Pairing code \(code.map(String.init).joined(separator: " "))")
                    Text("Check that \(peer) shows the same six digits.").font(.system(size: 11)).foregroundColor(Color(hex: "#8E939C")).lineLimit(2)
                }
                HStack(spacing: 8) {
                    SecondaryButton("No") { actions.answerPair(false) }.disabled(answered)
                    PrimaryButton(answered ? "Waiting for \(peer)" : "They match") { actions.answerPair(true) }.disabled(answered)
                }
            })
        }
    }

    private func offer(_ o: LinkOffer) -> some View {
        ZStack {
            CardBackground(wash: .cyan)
            pad(VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 5) {
                Text("\(o.peerName) wants to send you a file").font(.system(size: 12, weight: .semibold)).foregroundColor(Color(hex: "#F5F6F8")).lineLimit(1).truncationMode(.tail)
                // The name is the sanitized one: the one that would be saved.
                CodeBlock(text: "\(o.name)  \(LinkText.size(o.size))").lineLimit(1).truncationMode(.middle)
                Text("It is saved in Downloads/NotchMeow and is never opened for you. It is declined by itself if nobody answers in 2 minutes.").font(.system(size: 11)).foregroundColor(Color(hex: "#8E939C")).lineLimit(2)
                HStack(spacing: 8) {
                    SecondaryButton("Decline") { actions.respondOffer(false) }
                    PrimaryButton("Accept") { actions.respondOffer(true) }
                }
            })
        }
    }

    private func receiving(_ o: LinkOffer, _ got: UInt64) -> some View {
        ZStack {
            CardBackground(wash: .cyan)
            pad(VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 6) {
                Text("Receiving \(o.name) from \(o.peerName)").font(.system(size: 12, weight: .semibold)).foregroundColor(Color(hex: "#F5F6F8")).lineLimit(1).truncationMode(.middle)
                // Drawn, not a ProgressView: an AppKit-backed control cannot be drawn into the render script's pictures.
                GeometryReader { g in
                    ZStack(alignment: .leading) {
                        Capsule().fill(Color.white.opacity(0.10))
                        Capsule().fill(Color(hex: "#22D3EE")).frame(width: g.size.width * CGFloat(LinkText.percent(got, o.size)) / 100)
                    }
                }.frame(height: 6)
                HStack(spacing: 8) {
                    Text("\(LinkText.size(got)) of \(LinkText.size(o.size)), \(LinkText.percent(got, o.size)) %").font(.system(size: 11)).foregroundColor(Color(hex: "#8E939C")).monospacedDigit()
                    Spacer(minLength: 4)
                    SecondaryButton("Cancel") { actions.cancel() }
                }
            })
        }
    }

    private func result(_ title: String, _ detail: String, _ file: URL?) -> some View {
        ZStack {
            CardBackground(wash: file != nil ? .green : .soft)
            pad(VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 6) {
                Text(title).font(.system(size: 12, weight: .semibold)).foregroundColor(Color(hex: "#F5F6F8"))
                Text(detail).font(.system(size: 11.5)).foregroundColor(Color(hex: "#B9BDC4")).lineLimit(2)
                HStack(spacing: 8) {
                    if let file { SecondaryButton("Show in Finder") { actions.showInFinder(file) } }
                    PrimaryButton("OK") { actions.dismiss() }
                }
            })
        }
    }
}

/// Settings → Devices, as plain values: the app fills it from `LinkService` (`DevicesSettingsSection`), the render script from a stand-in.
struct DevicesSettingsView: View {
    var visible: Bool
    var deviceName: String
    var listening: Bool
    var problem: String?
    var note: String?
    var rows: [LinkDirectory.Row]
    var setVisible: (Bool) -> Void = { _ in }
    var rename: (String) -> Bool = { _ in true }
    var pair: (String) -> Void = { _ in }
    var forget: (String) -> Void = { _ in }
    var openLocalNetworkSettings: () -> Void = {}
    var showReceivedFolder: () -> Void = {}

    @State private var draft: String = ""
    @State private var nameRefused = false
    @State private var forgetting: String?

    var body: some View {
        VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 18) {
            GroupBox("This Mac") {
                VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 8) {
                    Toggle("Visible on this network", isOn: Binding(get: { visible }, set: { setVisible($0) }))
                    Text("Other Macs and PCs running NotchMeow can see this one and offer to pair. Until you turn this on, nothing listens and nothing is announced. Only machines you have paired can ever send a file.")
                        .font(.system(size: 11)).foregroundColor(.secondary).fixedSize(horizontal: false, vertical: true)
                    HStack(spacing: 8) {
                        Text("Name").frame(width: 44, alignment: .leading)
                        TextField("Name shown to other machines", text: $draft, onCommit: { nameRefused = !rename(draft) })
                            .textFieldStyle(.roundedBorder).frame(maxWidth: 260)
                    }
                    if nameRefused { Text("That name cannot be shown on another machine's card: use 1 to 63 characters, no control or invisible characters, no space at either end.").font(.system(size: 11)).foregroundColor(.orange) }
                    if visible { Text(listening ? "Listening on your network." : (problem ?? "Starting…")).font(.system(size: 11)).foregroundColor(problem == nil ? .secondary : .orange) }
                }
                .frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .leading).padding(6)
            }
            GroupBox("Machines") {
                VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 8) {
                    if !visible && rows.isEmpty {
                        Text("Turn on Visible on this network to find machines and pair with them.").font(.system(size: 12)).foregroundColor(.secondary)
                    } else if rows.isEmpty {
                        Text("No machines found").font(.system(size: 12)).foregroundColor(.secondary)
                        Text("Both machines need NotchMeow running, Visible on this network turned on, and to be on the same network. Some office and guest networks block this.").font(.system(size: 11)).foregroundColor(.secondary)
                    }
                    ForEach(rows, id: \.id) { row in
                        HStack(spacing: 8) {
                            Circle().fill(row.state == .pairedOffline ? Color.secondary.opacity(0.4) : (row.state == .pairedOnline ? Color.green : Color.blue)).frame(width: 7, height: 7)
                            Text(row.name).frame(width: 190, alignment: .leading).lineLimit(1)
                            Text(row.state == .pairedOnline ? "Paired" : row.state == .pairedOffline ? "Paired, not seen" : "Available").font(.system(size: 11)).foregroundColor(.secondary).frame(width: 110, alignment: .leading)
                            Spacer()
                            if row.state == .available { Button("Pair") { pair(row.id) } }
                            else if forgetting == row.id { Button("Forget \(row.name)?") { forget(row.id); forgetting = nil }.foregroundColor(.red); Button("Keep") { forgetting = nil } }
                            else { Button("Forget") { forgetting = row.id } }
                        }
                    }
                    if let note { Text(note).font(.system(size: 11)).foregroundColor(.orange) }
                    Button("Local Network permission…", action: openLocalNetworkSettings).font(.system(size: 11))
                    Text("If the list stays empty, check that NotchMeow is allowed under Privacy & Security, Local Network.").font(.system(size: 11)).foregroundColor(.secondary)
                }
                .frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .leading).padding(6)
            }
            GroupBox("Received files") {
                VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 8) {
                    Text("Files you accept are saved in Downloads/NotchMeow. They are marked as downloaded from elsewhere and are never opened for you.").font(.system(size: 11)).foregroundColor(.secondary).fixedSize(horizontal: false, vertical: true)
                    Button("Show in Finder", action: showReceivedFolder)
                }
                .frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .leading).padding(6)
            }
        }
        .onAppear { draft = deviceName }
    }
}

/// Settings → Devices in the app: the view above, filled from `LinkService`. Browsing for machines runs only while this section is on screen.
struct DevicesSettingsSection: View {
    @ObservedObject private var link = LinkService.shared

    var body: some View {
        DevicesSettingsView(visible: link.settings.visible, deviceName: link.settings.deviceName, listening: link.listening, problem: link.problem, note: link.note, rows: link.rows,
                            setVisible: { link.setVisible($0) }, rename: { link.setDeviceName($0) }, pair: { link.pair(with: $0) }, forget: { link.forget($0) },
                            openLocalNetworkSettings: { if let u = URL(string: "x-apple.systempreferences:com.apple.preference.security?Privacy_LocalNetwork") { NSWorkspace.shared.open(u) } },
                            showReceivedFolder: {
                                let folder = LinkFileSink.defaultFolder()
                                try? FileManager.default.createDirectory(at: folder, withIntermediateDirectories: true)
                                NSWorkspace.shared.activateFileViewerSelecting([folder])
                            })
            .accessibilityIdentifier(LinkService.marker)
            .onAppear { link.beginBrowsing(); link.devicesOpened() }
            .onDisappear { link.endBrowsing(); link.devicesClosed() }
    }
}
````

- [ ] **Step 5: Regenerate the project**

Run: `cd NotchBuddy && xcodegen && cd ..`
Expected: `Created project at …`. (The new Swift files are picked up from `Sources`; never edit the `.xcodeproj` by hand.)

- [ ] **Step 6: Build Debug**

Run: `(xcodebuild -project NotchBuddy/NotchBuddy.xcodeproj -scheme NotchBuddy -configuration Debug -derivedDataPath NotchBuddy/build build CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO > "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/g2-build-8.out" 2>&1; tail -3 "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/g2-build-8.out"; grep -c "warning:" "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/g2-build-8.out"; grep "Link.*warning:" "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/g2-build-8.out")`

### Task 10: The Accept card is an alert: the desktop cat, the hold-open, the pins

**Agent:** `nm-implementer-deep` (Sonnet 5.5, high; **trigger 1**: the island's alert path, the state machine's hold-open and the desktop cat's trip).

**Files:**
- Modify: `NotchBuddy/Sources/App/DesktopCatTrip.swift`, `DesktopCatController.swift`; `tests/DesktopCatTripTests.swift`, `tests/DesktopCatWiringTests.swift`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 8 (`AppState.linkOfferPending`, `AppState.linkHoldsIsland`, the hold-open clauses in `IslandWindowController`, `LinkService.card`), G2-0's `desktopAlert` flag `transferOffer` in `design/prototype/cat-core.js` and `desktop-cases.json`'s `offerNote`/`offerCases` (13 rows).
- Produces: `DesktopCatTrip.alert(…, transferOffer: Bool = false)` and its raw form that reads `transferOffer` (exactly `true` counts, as for every flag), and the controller passing `state.linkOfferPending`.

**What makes the Accept card an alert.** Three things, all already in place after Task 8 except the third: (1) `LinkService` posts `hookExpand` with `IslandView.link` when an offer arrives, which opens the island even when hidden (the same path an approval takes); (2) `AppState.linkHoldsIsland` is true while the card is up and is in both `fsm.isHeldOpen` closures, so the idle timer cannot fold it; (3) `desktopAlert` counts `transferOffer`, so the desktop cat flies to the notch and waits for the click. When the card goes (declined, expired, accepted into the progress card), `LinkService` clears the flag and, if the island is still on the `.link` view and nothing holds it, posts `islandCollapse`. The 2-minute decline is the receive run's one-shot timer, not a poll.

### Task 11: Send-menu integration: "To a device" in the shelf

**Agent:** `nm-implementer` (Haiku 5.5, high).

**Files:**
- Modify: `NotchBuddy/Sources/App/ShelfContent.swift`, `ShelfView.swift`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 8 (`LinkService.shared.rows`, `.settings.visible`, `.sendStatus`, `.send(_:to:model:)`, `beginBrowsing()`, `endBrowsing()`), G1's `ShelfButtons`, `ShelfBarButton`, `ShelfActionRules.canSend`.
- Produces: `ShelfDeviceChoice`, and the additive fields of `ShelfButtons` (`showDevicesButton`, `devices`, `sendToDevice`, `deviceLine`, `devicesHelp`).

**The decision (open question 1).** The spec says paired machines that are online come first in the Send menu. G1's Send is one button that opens the system's share picker, and it ships as it is. This task adds **one more button, icon only** (the bar has no room for a word: with "To a device" in words the render showed "Show in Finder" cut short, which would have restyled a shipped button), shown only when Visible is on or a machine is paired, so nobody who does not use the link sees a change. Clicking it replaces the bar's buttons with a row of chips: the paired machines, the online ones first and enabled, the others dimmed with "not on the network", then "Cancel"; with no paired machine it says "No paired machine yet" and a "Set up Devices" button that opens Settings → Devices. Choosing a machine sends the selected items to it, one offer for each, one after the other, each needing the other user's Accept. Status goes on the title row ("Waiting for Desk PC to accept a.pdf", "Sending a.pdf to Desk PC, 45 %", "Sent …", "… declined …"). The browser runs while the shelf is on screen, so "online" is current. Dragging an item onto a machine's chip (the spec's "can also be dragged") is **not** built here (open question 4).

### Task 12: The Debug fake peer

**Agent:** `nm-implementer` (Haiku 5.5, high).

**Files:**
- Create: `NotchBuddy/Sources/App/LinkDevPeer.swift`
- Modify: `NotchBuddy/Sources/App/AppDelegate.swift`; `NotchBuddy/NotchBuddy.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` (regenerated)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 8 (`LinkService.shared.debugInject(_:)`, `.listenerPort`, `.nowMs`), Tasks 4 to 7.
- Produces: `LinkDevPeer` (`marker` = `NM_LINK_DEV_PEER`, `start()`, `stop()`, `answerPair(_:)`, `offer(name:bytes:)`, `autoAccept`, `log`), `LinkDevView`, and the menu item "Link (Debug)…".

**What it is.** A second machine inside this Mac: an identity and a Keychain of its own **in memory**, a listener on 127.0.0.1 with no advert that puts itself on this Mac's list as "Debug Peer", and the same conversations as the real thing. The owner starts it, turns Visible on, clicks Pair next to Debug Peer, presses "They match" in the panel and in the island, then makes the fake peer offer a 3 MB file (the Accept card appears; accept it and the file lands in the real Downloads/NotchMeow), a 200 MB file (to cancel), or a name with a path in it (declined as not acceptable); and sends a shelf item to Debug Peer from "To a device" (the fake peer accepts by itself, into a temp folder; it is not a person, and it is compiled out of Release). Pairing it writes one Keychain entry named "Debug Peer", **under the Debug build's own service name** (`io.github.aikusan.notchmeow.debug`, never under the real one); Forget in Devices removes it. A fourth button, "Ask this Mac to pair", makes the fake peer start a pairing with this Mac: it is refused silently (no card; the panel says the connection ended without an answer) unless Devices is open here or you clicked Pair here in the last 2 minutes. The whole file is `#if DEBUG && !APPSTORE`, and `LinkService`'s debug hooks are too.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the fake peer and its menu item**

**Create `NotchBuddy/Sources/App/LinkDevPeer.swift`**

````swift
import AppKit
import CryptoKit
import SwiftUI
@preconcurrency import Network

#if DEBUG && !APPSTORE

/// The Debug-only fake peer (sub-project G2): a second machine inside this Mac, on the loopback address, so that a pairing, an Accept card, a transfer each way, a decline, a cancel and a refusal can be
/// tried without a second computer. It has an identity and a Keychain of its own **in memory**, listens on 127.0.0.1 without announcing itself, puts itself on this Mac's list of machines, and
/// speaks the same code as the real thing (LinkPairRun, LinkTransferReceive, LinkTransferSend over LinkNWTransport). Compiled in Debug builds only: `scripts/check-app-binary.sh` proves it is not in Release
/// or App Store builds (`marker`). Opened from the menu bar's "Link (Debug)…". Pairing it writes a real entry (named "Debug Peer") in this Mac's Keychain; Forget removes it.
@MainActor
final class LinkDevPeer: ObservableObject {
    static let marker = "NM_LINK_DEV_PEER"

    @Published private(set) var log: [String] = []
    @Published private(set) var running = false
    /// The code the fake peer shows while a pairing waits for its answer.
    @Published private(set) var code: String?
    /// Accept this peer's own incoming offers by itself (the fake peer is not a person; the real app never does this).
    @Published var autoAccept = true

    private let identity = LinkIdentity(deviceId: String(repeating: "dd", count: 16), name: "Debug Peer", key: Curve25519.KeyAgreement.PrivateKey())
    private let keychain = LinkKeychain(store: MemorySecretStore())
    private var listener: LinkListener?
    private var port: UInt16?
    private var pairRun: LinkPairRun?
    private var receiveRun: LinkTransferReceive?
    private var pairing = LinkPairing.ResponderState()
    let inbox = FileManager.default.temporaryDirectory.appendingPathComponent("NotchMeow-link-dev-\(UUID().uuidString)", isDirectory: true)

    func say(_ line: String) { log.append(line); if log.count > 10 { log.removeFirst(log.count - 10) } }

    func start() {
        guard listener == nil else { return }
        let l = LinkListener(onConnection: { [weak self] t, slot in Task { @MainActor in await self?.incoming(t, slot) } },
                             onState: { [weak self] port, failed in Task { @MainActor in self?.listenerState(port, failed) } })
        do { try l.start(advert: nil); listener = l } catch { say("could not listen: \(error)") }
    }

    func stop() {
        listener?.stop(); listener = nil; running = false
        LinkService.shared.debugInject([])
        say("stopped")
    }

    private func listenerState(_ p: UInt16?, _ failed: Bool) {
        guard let p, !failed else { if failed { say("the listener failed") }; return }
        port = p; running = true
        let ad = LinkWire.Advert(id: identity.deviceId, name: identity.name)
        LinkService.shared.debugInject([LinkBrowser.Found(advert: ad, endpoint: .hostPort(host: "127.0.0.1", port: NWEndpoint.Port(rawValue: p)!))])
        say("listening on 127.0.0.1:\(p); it is on this Mac's list as Debug Peer")
    }

    func answerPair(_ yes: Bool) { pairRun?.answer(yes); code = nil }

    private func incoming(_ t: LinkNWTransport, _ slot: LinkPendingSlot) async {
        guard let pre = try? LinkWire.decodePreamble(try await t.receive()) else { slot.finish(); await t.close(); return }
        switch pre.mode {
        case .pair:
            let run = LinkPairRun(transport: t, identity: identity, nonce: Data((0..<32).map { _ in UInt8.random(in: 0...255) }), clock: LinkService.nowMs,
                                  hooks: .init(showCode: { [weak self] c, n in Task { @MainActor in self?.code = c; self?.say("pairing with \(n): code \(c). Press They match here and in the island") } }, handshakeDone: { slot.finish() }))
            pairRun = run
            pairing.ids = LinkPairing.Ids(ownId: .value(identity.deviceId), paired: keychain.pairedKeys())
            let (outcome, next) = await run.runResponder(pairing)
            pairing = next; pairRun = nil; code = nil
            if case .paired(let peer) = outcome { try? keychain.add(peer, ownId: identity.deviceId); say("paired with \(peer.name)") } else { say("pairing ended: \(outcome)") }
        case .transfer:
            guard let peer = keychain.peer(pre.id) else { say("a transfer from a machine this peer does not know: closed"); slot.finish(); await t.close(); return }
            let run = LinkTransferReceive(transport: t, identity: identity, peer: peer, folder: inbox, hooks: .init(
                offered: { [weak self] o in Task { @MainActor in self?.say("offer: \(o.name), \(o.size) bytes"); if self?.autoAccept == true { self?.receiveRun?.respond(accept: true) } } },
                progress: { _, _ in }, claim: { _ in true }, release: { _ in }, handshakeDone: { slot.finish() }))
            receiveRun = run
            let outcome = await run.run()
            receiveRun = nil
            say("receiving ended: \(outcome)")
        }
    }

    /// The fake peer starts a pairing **with this Mac**: it is allowed only while Settings, Devices is open here, or within 2 minutes after you clicked Pair here (the owner's rule); at any other
    /// time this Mac refuses it silently, with no card, and the log says the connection ended without an answer.
    func pairWithMac() {
        guard let port = LinkService.shared.listenerPort else { say("this Mac is not listening: turn on Visible on this network in Settings, Devices"); return }
        Task {
            do {
                let transport = try await LinkNWTransport.ready(NWConnection(host: "127.0.0.1", port: NWEndpoint.Port(rawValue: port)!, using: .tcp), on: DispatchQueue(label: "notchmeow.link.devpeer.out"))
                let run = LinkPairRun(transport: transport, identity: identity, nonce: Data((0..<32).map { _ in UInt8.random(in: 0...255) }), clock: LinkService.nowMs,
                                      hooks: .init(showCode: { [weak self] c, n in Task { @MainActor in self?.code = c; self?.say("pairing with \(n): code \(c). Press They match here and in the island") } }))
                self.pairRun = run
                self.say("asking this Mac to pair")
                var state = LinkPairing.InitiatorState()
                state.ids = LinkPairing.Ids(ownId: .value(identity.deviceId), paired: keychain.pairedKeys())
                let (outcome, _) = await run.runInitiator(state)
                self.pairRun = nil; self.code = nil
                if case .paired(let peer) = outcome { try? keychain.add(peer, ownId: identity.deviceId); self.say("paired with \(peer.name)") }
                else if case .refused("handshake") = outcome { self.say("this Mac ended the connection without an answer (outside the gate: Devices closed, no recent Pair click)") }
                else { self.say("pairing ended: \(outcome)") }
            } catch { self.say("could not reach this Mac: \(error)") }
        }
    }

    /// The fake peer offers this Mac a file of `bytes` zeros: the real listener must be on (Visible on this network) and the two must be paired.
    func offer(name: String, bytes: Int) {
        guard let port = LinkService.shared.listenerPort else { say("this Mac is not listening: turn on Visible on this network in Settings, Devices"); return }
        guard let me = Optional(identity), let target = keychain.peers().values.first else { say("not paired yet: pair from Settings, Devices first"); return }
        try? FileManager.default.createDirectory(at: inbox, withIntermediateDirectories: true)
        let file = inbox.appendingPathComponent(name)
        try? Data(count: bytes).write(to: file)
        Task {
            do {
                let source = try LinkFileSource(url: file)
                let transport = try await LinkNWTransport.ready(NWConnection(host: "127.0.0.1", port: NWEndpoint.Port(rawValue: port)!, using: .tcp), on: DispatchQueue(label: "notchmeow.link.devpeer"))
                self.say("offering \(name) to this Mac")
                let outcome = await LinkTransferSend(transport: transport, identity: me, peer: target, source: source, hooks: .init(progress: { _, _ in })).run()
                self.say("offer ended: \(outcome)")
            } catch { self.say("could not offer: \(error)") }
        }
    }

    deinit { try? FileManager.default.removeItem(at: inbox) }
}

/// The panel: start and stop the fake peer, answer its side of a pairing, make it offer a file, and read what it did.
struct LinkDevView: View {
    @StateObject private var dev = LinkDevPeer()

    var body: some View {
        VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 12) {
            GroupBox("The fake peer (a second machine on 127.0.0.1)") {
                VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 8) {
                    HStack {
                        Button(dev.running ? "Stop" : "Start") { dev.running ? dev.stop() : dev.start() }
                        Toggle("It accepts its own incoming offers", isOn: $dev.autoAccept)
                    }
                    Text("Start it, turn on Visible on this network in Settings, Devices, and click Pair next to Debug Peer. This side shows the same code; press They match here and in the island.").font(.system(size: 11)).foregroundColor(.secondary)
                    HStack {
                        if let c = dev.code { Text(c).font(.system(size: 18, weight: .semibold, design: .monospaced)) }
                        Button("They match") { dev.answerPair(true) }.disabled(dev.code == nil)
                        Button("No") { dev.answerPair(false) }.disabled(dev.code == nil)
                    }
                }.padding(6)
            }
            GroupBox("Pair from this side") {
                HStack {
                    Button("Ask this Mac to pair") { dev.pairWithMac() }
                    Text("Refused silently unless Devices is open on this Mac, or you clicked Pair there in the last 2 minutes.").font(.system(size: 11)).foregroundColor(.secondary)
                }.padding(6)
            }
            GroupBox("Offer this Mac a file") {
                HStack {
                    Button("3 MB file") { dev.offer(name: "debug-3mb.bin", bytes: 3_000_000) }
                    Button("200 MB file (to cancel)") { dev.offer(name: "debug-200mb.bin", bytes: 200_000_000) }
                    Button("A name with a path in it") { dev.offer(name: "..:..:etc:passwd", bytes: 10) }
                }.padding(6)
            }
            GroupBox("What it did") {
                VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 3) {
                    ForEach(Array(dev.log.enumerated()), id: \.offset) { Text($0.element).font(.system(size: 11, design: .monospaced)) }
                    if dev.log.isEmpty { Text("Nothing yet").font(.system(size: 11)).foregroundColor(.secondary) }
                }.frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .leading).padding(6)
            }
        }.padding(16).frame(minWidth: 620, minHeight: 360)
    }
}

#endif
````

    private var linkPanelWindow: NSWindow?

    /// Debug builds only: the link's fake peer (sub-project G2), a second machine on the loopback address. Compiled out of Release and App Store builds; scripts/check-app-binary.sh proves it.
    @objc private func openLinkPanel() {
        if AppState.shared.mode == .expanded { islandController?.collapse() }
        if let w = linkPanelWindow, w.isVisible { w.makeKeyAndOrderFront(nil); NSApp.activate(ignoringOtherApps: true); return }
        let win = NSWindow(contentRect: NSRect(x: 0, y: 0, width: 660, height: 420), styleMask: [.titled, .closable, .miniaturizable], backing: .buffered, defer: false)
        win.title = "Link — NotchMeow (Debug)"
        win.identifier = NSUserInterfaceItemIdentifier(LinkDevPeer.marker)
        win.contentView = NSHostingView(rootView: LinkDevView())
        win.isReleasedWhenClosed = false
        placeBelowIsland(win)
        linkPanelWindow = win
        win.makeKeyAndOrderFront(nil)
        NSApp.activate(ignoringOtherApps: true)
    }

### Task 13: Entitlements, Info.plist and the binary check

**Agent:** `nm-implementer-light` (Haiku 5.5, medium).

**Files:**
- Modify: `NotchBuddy/project.yml`, `NotchBuddy/Resources/NotchMeowAppStore.entitlements`, `scripts/check-app-binary.sh`; `NotchBuddy/Resources/Info.plist`, `InfoAppStore.plist`, `NotchBuddy/NotchBuddy.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` (regenerated)

**What changes.** Both targets: `NSLocalNetworkUsageDescription` is reworded (one text now covers the AI model servers and the link, and says the link is used only if the user turns on Visible on this network) and `NSBonjourServices` lists `_notchmeow._tcp`. The App Store entitlements gain `com.apple.security.network.server` (the listener) and `com.apple.security.files.downloads.read-write` (a sandboxed app cannot reach `~/Downloads` without it; the owner chose this over a receive folder picked once). The GitHub build's entitlements do not change (it is not sandboxed). `check-app-binary.sh` gains the link's proofs: `NM_LINK_ENGINE_ACTIVE` is in every build, and `NM_LINK_DEV_PEER` and the Debug Keychain service string `io.github.aikusan.notchmeow.debug` are in a Debug build only (a Release or App Store binary that carries either fails the check).

**Found by the dry run:** the first marker, `NM_LINK_ACTIVE`, was 14 bytes: it showed in a Debug build and was **missing from the Release build**, because Swift keeps a string literal of 15 bytes or less inside the instruction that uses it, where `strings` cannot see it. Markers are 16 bytes or more (`NM_LINK_ENGINE_ACTIVE`, `NM_LINK_DEV_PEER`).

**What the pins say** (plain greps on the sources and the generated plists; a failing line names what moved): the stored default of Visible is false and a listener is started from three call sites only, each guarded; the advert is made in one place; the browser starts only from `beginBrowsing` and only when visible; there is no `Timer`, display link, `asyncAfter` or event monitor in the link, exactly four `Task.sleep` (the pairing window, an offer's 2 minutes, the connect timeout, the handshake watchdog) and none in `LinkService`; no literal accept anywhere, one `respond(accept:)` call that the Accept button reaches, and one function that turns a click into an accept event; every line the link logs is a fixed sentence in `link.log`; the two Keychain service names are the same text; nothing of the link writes a file but the sink; no private key leaves the Keychain and the handshake; a received file is created `O_EXCL | O_NOFOLLOW` private, and nothing else deletes; no network fetch but the sockets; **who may start a pairing** (the one gated function, the service's guard before anything runs, Devices telling the service when it is on screen, the Pair click opening the 2-minute window, the service never running a responder itself); the Keychain (the debug service name under `DEBUG && !APPSTORE` only, no other link file naming a service, the fake peer's keys in memory); the Accept card holds the island in both build variants, is an alert to the desktop cat and opens the island through `hookExpand`; the entitlements and plist keys are there for the App Store build and the GitHub build's entitlements did not change; the fake peer is Debug-only.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the pins**

**Create `scripts/test-link-wiring.sh`**

````bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Pins the call sites and the absences of the link (sub-project G2) in the app files no test program can compile: nothing listens until the user turns "Visible on this network" on, nothing polls,
# nothing accepts an offer but a click, no name reaches a log, the secrets live only in the Keychain, the Accept card is an alert like an approval, and the entitlements and Info.plist keys the
# App Store build needs are there. Plain greps on the sources and on the generated plists; a failing line says what moved.
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
APP=NotchBuddy/Sources/App
fail=0
pin() {   # pin "what it means" expected_count file... -- pattern
    local label="$1" want="$2"; shift 2
    local files=() pattern
    while [ "$1" != "--" ]; do files+=("$1"); shift; done
    shift; pattern="$1"
    local got
    got=$( (grep -h -E -- "$pattern" "${files[@]}" 2>/dev/null || true) | wc -l | tr -d ' ')
    if [ "$got" = "$want" ]; then echo "  ✓ $label"; else echo "  ✗ $label (found $got, expected $want of: $pattern)"; fail=$((fail + 1)); fi
}
LINK=($(ls $APP/Link*.swift | grep -v LinkDevPeer))     # every link file that is in a Release build
ALL=($(ls $APP/Link*.swift))

echo "off until the user turns it on"
pin "the stored default of Visible on this network is false" 1 $APP/LinkSettings.swift -- 'var visible = false'
pin "startListening is defined once and called from three places only: start() (when visible), setVisible(true) and a rename (when visible)" 4 $APP/LinkService.swift -- 'startListening\(\)'
pin "start() starts it only if settings.visible" 1 $APP/LinkService.swift -- 'if settings.visible \{ startListening\(\) \}'
pin "a rename restarts it only if visible" 1 $APP/LinkService.swift -- 'if settings.visible \{ stopListening\(\); startListening\(\) \}'
pin "the advert is made in one place (the listener's start), with the machine's id and name only" 1 $APP/LinkNetwork.swift -- 'NWTXTRecord\(\["v":'
pin "the browser is started only from beginBrowsing, and only when visible" 1 $APP/LinkService.swift -- 'guard browser == nil, settings.visible'
pin "the app starts the service once, at launch" 1 $APP/AppDelegate.swift -- 'LinkService\.shared\.start\(\)'

echo "who may start a pairing with this Mac"
pin "an incoming pairing goes through the one gated function" 1 $APP/LinkService.swift -- 'LinkIncomingPairing\.run\('
pin "the service checks the gate before it does anything for an incoming pairing" 1 $APP/LinkService.swift -- 'guard pairRun == nil, !pairingIncoming, pairGate\.allows\(now: Self\.nowMs\(\)\) else \{ slot\.finish\(\); await t\.close\(\); return \}'
pin "the gate is checked first in the function, before a run exists" 1 $APP/LinkPairRun.swift -- 'guard gate\.allows\(now: clock\(\)\) else \{ await transport\.close\(\); return nil \}'
pin "the service never starts a responder run itself" 0 $APP/LinkService.swift -- 'runResponder\('
pin "a responder run exists in three places only: its definition, the gated function and the fake peer's own side" 3 $APP/LinkPairRun.swift $APP/LinkDevPeer.swift $APP/LinkTransferRun.swift $APP/LinkNetwork.swift -- 'runResponder\('
pin "Devices tells the service when it is on screen" 1 $APP/LinkViews.swift -- 'link\.devicesOpened\(\)'
pin "and when it is not" 1 $APP/LinkViews.swift -- 'link\.devicesClosed\(\)'
pin "a click on Pair here opens the 2-minute window" 1 $APP/LinkService.swift -- 'pairGate\.pairClicked\(now: Self\.nowMs\(\)\)'

echo "nothing polls"
pin "no Timer, display link, dispatch timer, asyncAfter or event monitor in the link" 0 "${ALL[@]}" -- 'Timer|CADisplayLink|DispatchSourceTimer|asyncAfter|NSEvent\.add|scheduledTimer'
pin "the only sleeps are the pairing window, the offer's 2 minutes, the connect timeout and the handshake watchdog" 4 "${LINK[@]}" -- 'Task\.sleep'
pin "LinkService has no sleep of its own" 0 $APP/LinkService.swift -- 'Task\.sleep|sleep\('

echo "nothing accepts for the user"
pin "no literal accept in the service, the run or the network" 0 $APP/LinkService.swift $APP/LinkTransferRun.swift $APP/LinkNetwork.swift -- 'respond\(accept: true\)|respondOffer\(accept: true\)'
pin "the only call that accepts is the service's respondOffer, which the Accept button calls" 1 $APP/LinkService.swift -- 'receiveRun\?\.respond\(accept: accept\)'
pin "the Accept button is the only caller of respondOffer(true)" 1 $APP/LinkViews.swift -- 'actions\.respondOffer\(true\)'
pin "only the receive run turns a click into an accept event" 1 $APP/LinkTransferRun.swift -- 'nonisolated func respond\(accept: Bool\)'
pin "and it does so from that one function" 1 $APP/LinkTransferRun.swift -- 'post\.yield\(\.flow\(\.user\(accept \? \.accept : \.decline\)\)\)'

echo "no name in a log"
pin "every line the link logs is a fixed sentence" 0 "${ALL[@]}" -- 'appendAppLog\("link\.log", .*\\\('
other_logs=$( (grep -h 'appendAppLog(' "${ALL[@]}" || true) | (grep -v 'appendAppLog("link\.log"' || true) | wc -l | tr -d ' ')
if [ "$other_logs" = 0 ]; then echo "  ✓ the link logs through link.log only"; else echo "  ✗ the link logs somewhere else ($other_logs lines)"; fail=$((fail + 1)); fi
pin "no print or NSLog in the link" 0 "${LINK[@]}" -- '(^|[^a-zA-Z.])(print|NSLog|debugPrint)\('

echo "secrets only in the Keychain"
pin "the app's Keychain service name is the same text in both places" 1 $APP/ClaudeService.swift -- 'static let service = "io\.github\.aikusan\.notchmeow"$'
pin "and in the link, for Release and App Store builds" 1 $APP/LinkKeychain.swift -- 'static let appService = "io\.github\.aikusan\.notchmeow"$'
pin "a Debug build has a Keychain service of its own for the link, chosen at compile time" 1 $APP/LinkKeychain.swift -- 'static let appService = "io\.github\.aikusan\.notchmeow\.debug"$'
pin "that choice is under DEBUG and not APPSTORE" 1 $APP/LinkKeychain.swift -- '^    #if DEBUG && !APPSTORE$'
pin "no other link file names a Keychain service or reads the app's own Keychain wrapper" 0 $APP/LinkService.swift $APP/LinkViews.swift $APP/LinkSettings.swift $APP/LinkFiles.swift $APP/LinkNetwork.swift $APP/LinkChannel.swift $APP/LinkPairRun.swift $APP/LinkTransferRun.swift -- 'SystemSecretStore\(service|Keychain\.(save|load|service)|aikusan\.notchmeow'
pin "the fake peer keeps its keys in memory only" 1 $APP/LinkDevPeer.swift -- 'LinkKeychain\(store: MemorySecretStore\(\)\)'
pin "nothing of the link writes a file but the received file's sink" 0 "${LINK[@]}" -- '\.write\(to:|createFile\(|\.write\(contentsOf'
pin "the link stores nothing in UserDefaults but its two settings" 2 $APP/LinkSettings.swift -- 'store\.set\('
pin "no private key is turned into text or data outside the Keychain and the handshake" 0 $APP/LinkService.swift $APP/LinkViews.swift $APP/LinkFiles.swift -- 'rawRepresentation'

echo "files"
pin "a received file is created exclusively, never through a link, private" 1 $APP/LinkFiles.swift -- 'O_WRONLY \| O_CREAT \| O_EXCL \| O_NOFOLLOW \| O_CLOEXEC, 0o600'
pin "quarantine is set by hand on the GitHub build only" 1 $APP/LinkFiles.swift -- 'static let quarantineByHand = true'
pin "a failed transfer removes its partial file (discard), and nothing else deletes" 0 "${LINK[@]}" -- 'removeItem'
pin "the folder is Downloads/NotchMeow" 1 $APP/LinkFiles.swift -- 'appendingPathComponent\("NotchMeow", isDirectory: true\)'
pin "no network fetch anywhere in the link but the sockets" 0 "${ALL[@]}" -- 'URLSession|URLRequest|Data\(contentsOf: URL\(string'

echo "the Accept card is an alert like an approval"
pin "it holds the island open (both build variants)" 2 $APP/IslandWindowController.swift -- 'AppState\.shared\.linkHoldsIsland'
pin "it is an alert to the desktop cat" 1 $APP/DesktopCatController.swift -- 'transferOffer: state\.linkOfferPending'
pin "it opens the island even when hidden" 1 $APP/LinkService.swift -- 'name: \.hookExpand, object: IslandView\.link'
pin "the island knows the .link view" 1 $APP/IslandViewContent.swift -- 'case \.link:'
pin "Settings has a Devices section" 3 $APP/SettingsView.swift -- '"devices"'

echo "the build"
pin "the App Store entitlements ask for the server and Downloads" 2 NotchBuddy/Resources/NotchMeowAppStore.entitlements -- 'network\.server|files\.downloads\.read-write'
pin "the GitHub entitlements stay as they were (apple events only)" 1 NotchBuddy/Resources/NotchMeow.entitlements -- '<key>com\.apple\.security'
pin "both Info.plists announce _notchmeow._tcp" 2 NotchBuddy/Resources/Info.plist NotchBuddy/Resources/InfoAppStore.plist -- '<string>_notchmeow\._tcp</string>'
pin "both explain the local network use" 2 NotchBuddy/Resources/Info.plist NotchBuddy/Resources/InfoAppStore.plist -- 'Visible on this network'
pin "the project spec says the same for both targets" 2 NotchBuddy/project.yml -- '- _notchmeow\._tcp'
pin "the Debug fake peer is Debug-only" 1 $APP/LinkDevPeer.swift -- '^#if DEBUG && !APPSTORE'
pin "and its menu item too" 1 $APP/AppDelegate.swift -- 'Link \(Debug\)'

if [ "$fail" = 0 ]; then echo; echo "All tests passed."; else echo; echo "$fail test(s) failed."; exit 1; fi
````

````
The Mac port is G2-mac.
- Sub-project G2 on the Mac (between your machines): the pure ports, each replayed against `tests/fixtures/link/` by its own script, are `LinkNoise.swift` (Noise on CryptoKit only: `scripts/test-link-noise.sh`), `LinkBytes.swift`, `LinkName.swift` and `LinkFrames.swift` (`test-link-name.sh`, `test-link-frames.sh`), `LinkPairing.swift` and `LinkFlow.swift` (`test-link-pairing.sh`, `test-link-flow.sh`); `LinkKeychain.swift` keeps `link.static-key`, `link.device-id` and `link.peer.<id>` in the Keychain and `LinkSettings.swift` the two defaults (`link.visible`, **off until the user turns it on**; `link.deviceName`); `LinkFiles.swift` writes a received file to `~/Downloads/NotchMeow/` (exclusive create, quarantine, a partial file discarded). `LinkChannel.swift`, `LinkPairRun.swift` and `LinkTransferRun.swift` are the conversations (Swift 6 actors over a `LinkTransport`, tested between two in-memory ends by `test-link-session.sh`), `LinkNetwork.swift` the Network.framework transport, the listener with its Bonjour advert and the browser (`test-link-network.sh`, on 127.0.0.1), `LinkService.swift` the app's one holder of the link, `LinkViews.swift` the `.link` island cards and Settings → Devices (`render-link.sh` draws them). **Nothing listens or announces until Visible is on; an incoming pairing is allowed only while Devices is open or within 2 minutes after a Pair click here; the browser runs only while Send or Devices is open; nothing polls; nothing accepts an offer but a click, and a waiting Accept card is an alert (`transferOffer`) that opens the island and holds it.** The link is in every build (`NM_LINK_ENGINE_ACTIVE`; markers must be 16 bytes or more); the Debug fake peer `LinkDevPeer.swift` (`NM_LINK_DEV_PEER`) and the Debug Keychain service name (`io.github.aikusan.notchmeow.debug`) are compiled out of Release and App Store; the App Store build adds the `network.server` and `files.downloads.read-write` entitlements and the Bonjour plist keys. `scripts/test-link-wiring.sh` pins the absences and the call sites.
````

A conversation (`LinkPairRun`, `LinkTransferReceive`, `LinkTransferSend`) is one `run()` that consumes one `AsyncStream` of events (the peer's frames, the user's click, a one-shot timer) and lets the pure machines (`LinkPairing`, `LinkFlow`) decide; it speaks to a `LinkTransport` (in memory in the tests, `LinkNWTransport` on Network.framework in the app). `LinkService` is the only file that joins them to the app: the listener and its Bonjour advert exist only while `link.visible` is on, the browser only while Settings → Devices or the shelf is on screen, an incoming pairing runs only while Devices is open or within 2 minutes after a Pair click here (otherwise refused silently, no card), an unknown device id is closed, and an offer becomes the `.link` island card that only a click answers and that holds the island open and calls the desktop cat (`transferOffer`).

### La garde-robe
````

Replace with:

````
### Entre vos machines (sous-projet G2)

Envoyer des fichiers d'un Mac à un autre Mac ou à un PC Windows/Linux qui font tourner NotchMeow, sur le réseau local. Le contrat : `docs/LINK-CONTRACT.md` ; la spec : `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-04-file-shelf-transfer-design.md` ; le plan Mac : `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-15-g2-mac-link.md`.

- **Visible sur ce réseau** (Réglages → Appareils) : **désactivé par défaut**. Tant que ce n'est pas activé, rien n'écoute, rien n'est annoncé, aucune invite de réseau local. Une fois activé, ça le reste. Le nom affiché aux autres machines est modifiable.
- **Jumelage** : une machine lance « Pair » depuis Réglages → Appareils, l'autre montre la carte « Pair with … ? » **seulement si Réglages → Appareils est ouvert chez elle, ou dans les 2 minutes après un clic sur « Pair a machine » chez elle** ; sinon la demande est refusée sans bruit (rien n'est envoyé, la connexion se ferme, ni carte ni alerte). Les deux écrans montrent les mêmes six chiffres (engagement puis révélation, voir le contrat) et chacun clique « They match ». Une seule tentative toutes les 30 s ; la fenêtre dure 2 minutes. Les clés vont dans le Trousseau (`link.static-key`, `link.peer.<id>`), jamais sur le disque.
- **Envoi** : le bouton « To a device » de l'étagère (icône seule) liste les machines jumelées, celles en ligne d'abord. Un fichier à la fois, chacun avec sa propre carte chez l'autre.
- **Carte d'acceptation** : « <machine> wants to send you a file » avec le nom nettoyé et la taille ; **seul un clic accepte**. C'est une alerte comme une demande d'autorisation : elle ouvre l'île même cachée, la retient ouverte, et le chat de bureau vole vers le notch. Sans réponse en 2 minutes, l'offre est refusée toute seule.
- **Réception** : `~/Downloads/NotchMeow/`, jamais ouvert par l'app, marqué en quarantaine, nom numéroté en cas de collision, fichier partiel supprimé si le transfert s'interrompt. Limites : 4 Go, un transfert par machine.
- **Build App Store** : tout y est ; il ajoute les droits `network.server` et `files.downloads.read-write` et les clés `NSBonjourServices` / `NSLocalNetworkUsageDescription`. Debug : « Link (Debug)… » (un faux pair sur 127.0.0.1, marqueur `NM_LINK_DEV_PEER`, absent de Release et App Store) ; un build Debug garde les clés du lien sous son propre service Trousseau (`io.github.aikusan.notchmeow.debug`), absent de Release et App Store.
- Code : `LinkNoise`, `LinkName`, `LinkFrames`, `LinkPairing`, `LinkFlow`, `LinkKeychain`, `LinkSettings`, `LinkFiles`, `LinkChannel`, `LinkPairRun`, `LinkTransferRun`, `LinkNetwork`, `LinkService`, `LinkViews`, `LinkDevPeer`.

### After Task 14: the whole-branch review

**Agent:** `nm-reviewer-deep` (Sonnet 5.5, high; trigger 4: a whole-branch review that includes the **security pass** the spec asks for). Read-only. It loads `ponytail:ponytail-review` and `superpowers:requesting-code-review`, and reads `git diff "$(git merge-base HEAD notchmeow/rebrand)"...HEAD`.

It checks, and reports with file and line:
1. **The contract against the ports.** Each `Link*` port against `docs/LINK-CONTRACT.md` and `tests/fixtures/link/reference.py` (not only against the fixtures): the Noise handshake (pre-messages for `KK`, the starting hash, the nonce layout, a refused tag not moving the counter, the DH zero check, the size limits), the pairing machines' order of checks and the commit-then-reveal order in `LinkPairRun`, `peerRefusal`, the frame decoders' strictness, the sanitizer's order of steps and its hand-written tables (and a note of which Unicode data comes from the system: categories, NFC and NFKC, case folding), the receiver's and sender's machines, `planChunks`.
2. **The security pass.** The Noise implementation: no key or ciphertext logged, ephemeral keys random and never reused, no secret in an error message, `Data` slices re-based before indexing; the frame parser: no trap on any input (the 8000-frame fuzz is the floor, not the ceiling), no unbounded buffer (`LinkNWTransport` holds at most one record and a chunk of the stream); the file-name sanitizer and the sink: nothing is written before the user's click, `O_EXCL`/`O_NOFOLLOW`, no path built from the raw name, a partial file always discarded (every `return` of `LinkTransferReceive.run` goes through `finish`); the pairing code's strength (six digits, one guess per attempt, the commitment checked before the code, the rate limit and the replay memory kept across connections by `LinkService`); the Keychain items (accessibility attribute, a corrupt item never replaced, the paired map re-read at store time); the listener (nothing before Visible, the 10 s deadline and the 8 pending, an unknown device id closed without an answer, one pairing at a time, no information leaked in the closing behaviour beyond the contract's documented limit).
3. **Concurrency.** Swift 6: no `nonisolated(unsafe)` outside tests, no unchecked `Sendable` beyond the three documented wrappers (`LinkSink`, `LinkPendingSlot`, the listener), every `Task` that touches the service hops to the main actor, no actor method that can deadlock (`LinkChannel`'s lock is released on every path), every `AsyncStream` consumer ends and cancels its reader and timers.
4. **Privacy and performance.** No telemetry, no name or address in a log, no listener before Visible, no polling (the wiring script and a read of every `Task.sleep`), the browser stopped when Devices and the shelf close.
5. **The shipping paths.** `git diff` of `UploadCanvasView.swift`, `UploadSequenceEngine.swift`, `UploadCatDriver.swift`, `CatFileRule.swift`, `FileDropView.swift` and `IslandWing.swift` is empty; the shelf's bar differs only by the icon-only button, the chips and the status line (an empty title is an icon alone, and no existing button's text changes); the existing Settings rows and the island's views are unchanged.
6. **The Review Focus** (five items above): each has a test, and the test fails when the behaviour is broken (run one mutation of each).

Fix what it finds (a fix round is a small `nm-implementer` task, then a scoped re-review by `nm-reviewer`), then go to Task 15.

- [ ] **Step 3: The owner's real-build checklist (the owner runs it; no agent launches an app)**

Build the Debug app from the branch (or the tester zip, `scripts/release.sh <version> --tester`) and run it **on your Mac**. Items 1 to 9 need only this Mac (the fake peer); items 10 to 12 need a second machine and wait for G2-tauri's app (or a second Mac running this branch). In order:

1. **Nothing until you ask.** Fresh launch (or after clearing `link.visible`): Settings → Devices shows "Visible on this network" **off**. In Activity Monitor or `lsof -i -P | grep NotchMeow` nothing listens, and macOS has not shown a Local Network prompt. Quit and reopen: still off.
2. **Turning it on.** Switch it on: the Local Network prompt appears once (the text mentions both the AI servers and your other machines); allow it. The status line says "Listening on your network." Switch it off: the listener goes (`lsof` again).
3. **The prompt declined.** (Reset the permission in System Settings first, then decline.) Devices says the Mac could not listen and the "Local Network permission…" button opens the right System Settings pane.
4. **The fake peer.** Menu bar → "Link (Debug)…" → Start. With Visible on, Devices lists "Debug Peer" as Available. (First, with **Settings closed**, press "Ask this Mac to pair" in the panel: nothing appears in the island, the desktop cat stays put, and the panel says the connection ended without an answer. Then open Settings → Devices and press it again: the card appears.) Click Pair: a card "Pair with Debug Peer?" shows six digits in the island, and the panel shows the same six. Press "They match" in both: the island says Paired, Devices lists Debug Peer as Paired. Press "No" on one side in a second try (wait 30 s between tries: "Wait 30 seconds before trying again." must appear if you do not).
5. **A file from the peer.** In the panel, "3 MB file": the island opens **even if it was hidden**, the Accept card names `debug-3mb.bin` and 3 MB, and **nothing is written** until you click. Click Accept: progress, then "Received" with "Show in Finder"; the file is in `~/Downloads/NotchMeow/`, and `xattr -l` on it shows `com.apple.quarantine`. Offer it again: `debug-3mb (1).bin`.
6. **The alert.** With the desktop cat out (⌃⌥D), offer a file: the cat flies to the notch and waits; decline: it goes back. Offer one and do nothing: after 2 minutes the card says it expired and nothing is saved. While the card is up, the island does not fold by itself.
7. **Cancel and refuse.** Offer the 200 MB file, accept, click Cancel: "Transfer cancelled", and the partial file is gone from Downloads/NotchMeow. Offer "A name with a path in it": declined as not acceptable, nothing saved.
8. **The window after a click.** Click Pair next to Debug Peer, answer No, close Settings, and press "Ask this Mac to pair" within 2 minutes (after 30 s): the card appears; press it again after the 2 minutes are over: refused silently. Check Keychain Access: the link's items are under `io.github.aikusan.notchmeow.debug`, and none was made under `io.github.aikusan.notchmeow` by this build.
9. **A file to the peer.** Put a file on the shelf, select it, click the laptop icon, choose Debug Peer: the title row says it is waiting, then sending, then "Sent". With Debug Peer stopped, its chip is dimmed. Forget Debug Peer in Devices (second click confirms) and check Keychain Access has no "link.peer" item left for it under the debug service.
10. **A real second machine** (a second Mac on this branch; later a PC): both visible, pair with matching codes, and a wrong code (decline on one side) stores nothing on either.
11. **Both ways.** Send a file each way, each needing Accept; send three files at once (three cards, one after the other); Decline one; send a file bigger than 4 GB (refused as too large); turn off Wi-Fi mid-transfer (both sides stop, nothing partial remains).
12. **The App Store build** (sandboxed; build it with the `NotchMeowAppStore` scheme and run it): Visible on prompts for local network; pair; a received file lands in `~/Downloads/NotchMeow/` (the Downloads entitlement), quarantined by the sandbox.
13. **CPU.** With the link on and the island hidden, Activity Monitor shows NotchMeow at 0 % while idle.

Write the answers in the report: each line passes, fails, or needs a change.

---

### Task 16: Cross-app loopback check (Swift and Rust): waits for G2-tauri

**Agent:** none yet. **Not run in this plan's session.** The spec's cross-app check ("on one Mac, the Swift link code and the Rust link code pair over loopback, then send files both ways, verified by SHA-256") needs the Rust side, which is G2-tauri's, and Docker (the Rust build) is down. This task is written here so that it is not forgotten; it is run by whichever session first has both sides.

What it will do, so that the two plans can be built to meet it:

1. **A Swift test binary with no app in it.** A small program that compiles the same `Link*` files this plan's tests compile (`LinkNoise` to `LinkTransferRun` and `LinkNetwork`, none of the SwiftUI or service files) and runs a role chosen on the command line: `pair-initiator`, `pair-responder`, `send FILE`, `receive DIR`. It reads its identity and its paired machine from a JSON file (the keys are plain test patterns, never real ones) and prints the six digits and the SHA-256 of what it received. It uses `LinkListener` without an advert and `LinkNWTransport`, so there is no Bonjour and no prompt.
2. **The Rust test binary** (G2-tauri's, a module with no Tauri types) speaks the same roles on the same port.
3. **The script** (`scripts/test-link-crossapp.sh`, added then) starts one binary as responder and the other as initiator on 127.0.0.1, checks that both print **the same six digits**, auto-answers "They match" on both, then sends a file each way (Swift to Rust, Rust to Swift) with an accept given by the test (the test's own receiver, never the app's), and compares SHA-256. It also runs the cross cases the fixtures cannot: a `KK` handshake with the wrong stored key (both sides refuse), a tampered record, and a file name from the Rust side that the Mac's sanitizer rewrites the way the contract says.
4. **The pass criterion.** Both directions, both roles, the same digits, equal hashes, and the damaged cases refused. If the two ever disagree, `tests/fixtures/link/reference.py` decides which is wrong.

Until G2-tauri exists the contract's fixtures are the only cross-check: both ports pass the same vectors, which is why Task 1 runs `tests/link-crosscheck/xcheck.swift` too.
