## The owner's answers (2026-10-15)

The owner answered the ten open questions of the first draft. Two answers changed the plan (2 and 8); the other eight are the recommended options as drafted. Every one is built as written above.

1. **Where "To a device" lives.** Answer: the recommended option. One additive, icon-only button in the shelf's bar, shown only when Visible is on or a machine is paired, that opens a row of chips. G1's Send and AirDrop buttons stay exactly as they are (Task 11).
2. **Who may pop a card on this Mac. Changed.** The pairing card is shown **only while Settings → Devices is open on this Mac, or within 2 minutes after the user clicked "Pair a machine" on this Mac.** At any other time an incoming pairing hello is refused silently, with the same wire behaviour as other hello refusals (nothing sent, the connection closes), with no card and no alert. Visible stays off by default. Built as `LinkPairing.Gate` (Task 3: its edges, open at the click, open 1 ms before 2 minutes, closed at exactly 120 s, a clock that went back or a missing clock refused), `LinkIncomingPairing.run` (Task 6: outside the gate nothing is sent and no card is shown; inside it the pairing runs), `LinkService.devicesOpened()`/`devicesClosed()` and `pairClicked` (Task 8), the fake peer's "Ask this Mac to pair" button (Task 12) and the wiring pins (Task 14).
3. **Where the device id and the name live.** Answer: the recommended option. The id in the Keychain next to the key (`link.device-id`), the name and Visible in UserDefaults.
4. **Dragging a shelf item onto a machine.** Answer: the recommended option. Not built in this plan; the chips are buttons. It can be a follow-up task.
5. **Several files to one machine.** Answer: the recommended option. One offer for each file, one after the other, each needing the other user's Accept; no batch frame (the contract is unchanged).
6. **After a file is received.** Answer: the recommended option. The card offers "Show in Finder" and "OK"; nothing is opened.
7. **The listener's own limits.** Answer: the recommended option. A connection that has not finished its handshake in 10 s is cut and at most 8 wait at once; G2-tauri may choose its own.
8. **The Debug fake peer and the real Keychain. Changed.** The Debug build stores **all** link keys and the device id under its own Keychain service name, `io.github.aikusan.notchmeow.debug` (the normal service plus `.debug`), chosen at compile time under `#if DEBUG && !APPSTORE` in `SystemSecretStore`; Release and App Store builds use the normal `io.github.aikusan.notchmeow`. `scripts/check-app-binary.sh` proves the debug string is **absent** from Release and App Store binaries (and present in a Debug one). No test or dev path ever writes under the real service name: the Keychain test uses a service made for its run and checks that it is neither of the two, and the fake peer's own keys are in memory (Tasks 4, 12 and 13, and a wiring pin in Task 14).
9. **Quarantine on the partial file.** Answer: the recommended option. In the GitHub build the quarantine attribute is set when the file is created, so even a partial file is quarantined, and an incomplete file is deleted.
10. **The CI Keychain step.** Answer: the recommended option. CI runs the Keychain test on a memory store only (`NM_SKIP_REAL_KEYCHAIN=1`); the real-Keychain round trip is run by the agent and by the owner.

## Dry run

Before this plan was handed over, the plan was applied for real in a throwaway clone (`git clone --local --shared --branch notchmeow/rebrand`, at `6adc5a4`, under the session's scratchpad), in the order it gives, and the code in the tasks above is the code that ran. What ran, and what it showed:

- **Task 1 (Noise).** `test-link-noise.sh`: 38 ticks, all four vectors (every ciphertext and the handshake hash), all twenty damaged-message cases (stopping at `failsAt`, the two zero-point cases refused as such), the size limits, 24 random sessions. The independent `tests/link-crosscheck/xcheck.swift` agrees. **Fixed in the plan:** the first draft trapped on a `Data` slice (`box.ciphertext + box.tag` of an empty message does not start at index 0).
- **Task 2 (names, frames).** 162 name cases and 14 collision cases, 6000 + 3000 fuzz names, every frame/reject/limit/hello/advert/preamble/record case and an 8000-frame fuzz: all pass first time on Foundation's NFC, NFKC, categories and `folding(options: .caseInsensitive)` (ß folds to ss, as the contract's known limit says). **Fixed in the plan:** the fixture's byte-order-mark name was lost by `JSONSerialization` (it drops a leading BOM in a string), so `filename-cases.json` is read with `JSONDecoder`.
- **Task 3 (machines).** 47 responder rows, 27 initiator rows, 5 whole pairings, 50 receiver rows, 16 sender rows, 9 whole transfers, the clocks' 29 boundary rows and a 3000-script fuzz: pass. **Fixed:** a `last` that is given but not whole milliseconds must be refused, not read as "no attempt yet" (the bridge maps it to -1).
- **Task 4 (Keychain, settings).** Memory store and the real Keychain under `io.github.aikusan.notchmeow.test.link.<uuid>`: 17 ticks, the items made by the run were deleted and the test checked that none remained; no prompt appeared. **Fixed:** `SystemSecretStore` carries the app's service name itself (`appService`) instead of calling `Keychain.service`, so the test does not need `ClaudeService.swift`; the wiring script pins that the two are the same text.
- **Task 5 (files).** 14 ticks including the quarantine attribute read back with `getxattr`, a link with the wanted name taken and not followed, and a file made between the listing and the create that is not overwritten.
- **Task 6 (conversations).** 22 ticks in about 5 s (26 after the revision below). **Fixed in the plan, all three real bugs of the first draft:** records were length-framed twice (the first record never decoded and the initiator hung: the transport contract is now stated and the memory end enforces the record length); a cancel click while an offer waits is *ignored* by the contract (my first test expected a decline and hung); an in-memory transfer finishes before a click arrives, so the cancel-in-the-middle test paces the sender. The program carries a 90 s watchdog and prints unbuffered so a hang shows how far it got.
- **Task 7 (network).** Real TCP on 127.0.0.1 through a `LinkListener` without an advert: records, the 65535/65536 limit, a record of length 0, a whole pairing, and the 10 s silent-connection cut: all pass, about 15 s, and no Local Network prompt appeared. Bonjour (the advert and the browser) was **not** exercised: it needs the owner's real build or the fake peer.
- **Tasks 8 to 13 (the app).** `xcodegen` and a Debug build with `CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO`: **BUILD SUCCEEDED**; the only warning in a `Link` file (an implicit strong capture under `[weak self]`) was fixed in the code above. Release and App Store builds: BUILD SUCCEEDED. `check-app-binary.sh`: Debug, Release and App Store all pass the link's markers. **Fixed in the plan:** the first marker (`NM_LINK_ACTIVE`, 14 bytes) was missing from the Release binary (short Swift string literals are not found by `strings`); it is `NM_LINK_ENGINE_ACTIVE` now and the rule is in the Global Constraints. Two existing wiring pins (G1's hold-open line and the desktop cat's alert line) broke because the plan edits the lines they quote; the plan edits the pins in the same tasks (8 and 10). `render-link.sh` drew the three sheets; **fixed after looking at them:** `ProgressView` renders as a yellow bar with a forbidden sign (drawn bar now), `GroupBox` renders as bare titles under `ImageRenderer` (the devices sheet goes through a hosting view), and a worded "To a device" button cut "Show in Finder" short (the button is icon-only).
- **Task 10.** `test-cat-desktop-trip.sh` replays the 13 `offerCases` through the new flag. **Task 14.** `test-link-wiring.sh`: 40 pins, all pass.
- **Revision of 2026-10-15 (the owner's answers 2 and 8), re-run in the same clone.** `LinkPairing.Gate` and its edges pass in `test-link-pairing.sh`. `test-link-session.sh` has a new section that runs an incoming pairing through `LinkIncomingPairing.run` four ways (gate closed: no run, no card, **zero records sent**, initiator refused; a click 120 s ago, at exactly 120 s: refused; at 119 999 ms: runs and shows the card; Devices open: runs at any time) and now passes with 26 ticks. **Found and fixed:** the first version of that section hung, because a pairing that is inside the gate runs for real and waits for two users; the test now cancels both users once the card is up, and keeps its 90 s watchdog. `test-link-wiring.sh` has 52 pins (the gate, the debug service). All three builds (Debug, Release, App Store) were rebuilt and `check-app-binary.sh` re-run: the link marker in all three, the fake peer and the debug service string present in Debug and absent from Release and App Store. The Keychain test, run again on the real Keychain, made and deleted its items under its own service only.
- **Not run in the dry run:** the commit steps; Tasks 15 and 16; anything that needs the app running, Bonjour, a second machine, or a click. The dry run never launched the app, never ran `claude`, and added items to the real Keychain only under the test-only service name, which the test deleted again.
